无论如何，我最终打定主意，务要研读四福音书，无论发现哪个教派恪守福音书之训导，我便加入该派。我就这样步履维艰地摸索前行，毫无明师指引我迈向永生之道。我向形形色色之人讨教求取天国之法，得到的答案却五花八门。我困惑动摇不已，在当时竟找不出一个比我自己更为公义、或比我更热心敬虔之人。我想我们断不可能尽皆得救（这与圣经经文相合），亦不可能尽皆沉沦。在我所结识的圈子里，竟无一人能全然持守十诫。我在自己眼中是何等自以为义，深信自己在这一点上远胜众人，因为十诫之中我守全了八条；而眼见那些自诩基督徒之人普遍不如土耳其人（Turks）诚实良善，我甚至真切地以为，土耳其人走向得救的道路远比我身边的邻舍更为稳妥：于是我在希望与恐惧之间苦苦挣扎度日，所能享受的至大慰藉，不过是当时练习吹奏的法国圆号，以及学习理发理容的手艺。我在此等境遇下熬过了数月，饱尝了此地许多人的虚伪狡诈。我最终决意前往土耳其（Turkey），并在彼处了此残生。当时正值 1774 年初春。我四处寻觅东家，结识了约翰·休斯船长（Captain John Hughes），他统领一艘名为“英格兰尼亚号”（Anglicania）的海船，正在泰晤士河整修备航，即将开赴土耳其的士麦那（Smyrna）。我应聘登船充当管事侍从；同时我还向他举荐了一名极为干练的黑人约翰·安尼斯（John Annis）充任厨师。此人在船上恪尽职守近 2 个月：他早年曾侍奉圣基茨岛（St. Kitts）绅士威廉·柯克帕特里克先生（Mr. William Kirkpatrick）多年，双方系协议分手，然其后这位前主人却屡设圈套企图诱捕这可怜人。他曾多方串通前往圣基茨的船长诱拐安尼斯；待所有诱捕绑架之阴谋尽告破产后，柯克帕特里克先生探得安尼斯在船上，竟于 4 月 4 日复活节星期一（Easter Monday），纠集 2 艘单橹小艇和 6 名男子闯至联合阶梯码头（Union Stairs）扑向我船；在大副与全体船员眼前，将安尼斯五花大绑强行掳出海船，而那大副此前在得知对方来捕的风声后，竟故意将安尼斯扣留在船上不许走脱。

===Original===

I believe that this was a combined piece of business: but, at any rate, it certainly reflected great disgrace on the mate and captain also, who, although they had desired the oppressed man to stay on board, yet he did not in the least assist to recover him, or pay me a farthing of his wages, which was about five pounds. I proved the only friend he had, who attempted to regain him his liberty if possible, having known the want of liberty myself. I sent as soon as I could to Gravesend, and got knowledge of the ship in which he was; but unluckily she had sailed the first tide after he was put on board. My intention was then immediately to apprehend Mr. Kirkpatrick, who was about setting off for Scotland; and, having obtained a *habeas corpus* for him, and got a tipstaff to go with me to St. Paul’s churchyard, where he lived, he, suspecting something of this kind, set a watch to look out. My being known to them occasioned me to use the following deception: I whitened my face, that they might not know me, and this had its desired effect. He did not go out of his house that night, and next morning I contrived a well plotted stratagem notwithstanding he had a gentleman in his house to personate him. My direction to the tipstaff, who got admittance into the house, was to conduct him to a judge, according to the writ. When he came there, his plea was, that he had not the body in custody, on which he was admitted to bail. I proceeded immediately to that philanthropist, Granville Sharp, Esq. who received me with the utmost kindness, and gave me every instruction that was needful on the occasion. I left him in full hope that I should gain the unhappy man his liberty, with the warmest sense of gratitude towards Mr. Sharp for his kindness; but, alas! my attorney proved unfaithful; he took my money, lost me many months employ, and did not do the least good in the cause: and when the poor man arrived at St. Kitts, he was, according to custom, staked to the ground with four pins through a cord, two on his wrists, and two on his ankles, was cut and flogged most unmercifully, and afterwards loaded cruelly with irons about his neck. I had two very moving letters from him, while he was in this situation; and also was told of it by some very respectable families now in London, who saw him in St.

===Chinese===

我深信这是一场里应外合的肮脏勾当：但无论如何，这无疑给大副乃至船长带来了莫大的耻辱，彼等虽曾要求这受屈之人留船效力，事后却未作丝毫营救之举，更不肯将他约 5 英镑的工钱分文付我转交。我因亲身体认过失去自由之惨痛，成了唯一挺身而出全力图谋为他重获自由的朋友。我尽可能迅速遣人赶往格雷夫森德（Gravesend），查清了他被押送登上的海船；厄运弄人，该船在将他押入舱底后的第一波潮汐中便扬帆启航了。我当即决意扣押正欲启程前往苏格兰的柯克帕特里克先生；我为他办妥了人身保护令（habeas corpus），并延请法警（tipstaff）随我同往其居住的圣保罗教堂大院（St. Paul's churchyard）；彼疑心生暗鬼，早早布下暗哨窥探防范。因门卫认得我的面孔，我便施略施小计：将自己的脸庞敷得惨白，使他们难以辨识，此计果然奏效。当夜他闭门不出，次日晨我布设了一套周密的计策，尽管他在宅中安排了一名绅士冒名顶替，法警依然闯入宅内，依令将其押解至法官面前听候传讯。到了法官席前，他的辩词竟是那黑人并不在其人身扣押之下，法官因之准予其取保候审。我随即疾奔那位慈善家格兰维尔·夏普阁下（Granville Sharp, Esq.）门下，他极尽仁慈地接纳了我，并就当务之急赐予我一切详尽指引。辞别夏普先生时，我满怀着为那不幸伙伴重获自由的希冀，心中对夏普先生的善德充满无尽感恩；然而呜呼哀哉！我所委托的律师背信弃义；他榨干了我的钱财，耽搁了我数月生计，却未在这桩申冤义举中尽半点心力：而当那可怜人被押抵圣基茨岛后，竟按恶俗被 4 枚木桩牢牢钉死在地上，手腕脚踝各穿绳索固定于 2 桩之上，遭受了惨无人道的残酷抽挞，其后颈项更被套上沉重的铁镣。在此惨境中，我曾收到他寄来的 2 封字字泣血的书信；几户如今客居伦敦的体面人家亦向我证实了此事，他们曾在圣基茨岛亲眼目睹他的惨状。

===Original===

Kitts, in the same state in which he remained till kind death released him out of the hands of his tyrants. During this disagreeable business I was under strong convictions of sin, and thought that my state was worse than any man’s; my mind was unaccountably disturbed; I often wished for death, though at the same time convinced I was altogether unprepared for that awful summons. Suffering much by villains in the late cause, and being much concerned about the state of my soul, these things (but particularly the latter) brought me very low; so that I became a burden to myself, and viewed all things around me as emptiness and vanity, which could give no satisfaction to a troubled conscience. I was again determined to go to Turkey, and resolved, at that time, never more to return to England. I engaged as steward on board a Turkeyman (the *Wester Hall*, Capt. Linna); but was prevented by means of my late captain, Mr. Hughes, and others. All this appeared to be against me, and the only comfort I then experienced was, in reading the holy scriptures, where I saw that “there is no new thing under the sun,” Eccles. 1:9; and what was appointed for me I must submit to. Thus I continued to travel in much heaviness, and frequently murmured against the Almighty, particularly in his providential dealings; and, awful to think! I began to blaspheme, and wished often to be anything but a human being. In these severe conflicts the Lord answered me by awful “visions of the night, when deep sleep falleth upon men, in slumberings upon the bed,” Job 33:15. He was pleased, in much mercy, to give me to see, and in some measure to understand, the great and awful scene of the judgment-day, that “no unclean person, no unholy thing, can enter into the kingdom of God,” Eph. 5:5. I would then, if it had been possible, have changed my nature with the meanest worm on the earth; and was ready to say to the mountains and rocks “fall on me,” Rev. 6:16; but all in vain. I then requested the divine Creator that he would grant me a small space of time to repent of my follies and vile iniquities, which I felt were grievous. The Lord, in his manifold mercies, was pleased to grant my request, and being yet in a state of time, the sense of God’s mercies was so great on my mind when I awoke, that my strength entirely failed me for many minutes, and I was exceedingly weak.

===Chinese===

他就这般深陷于圣基茨（St. Kitts）的绝境，直至仁慈的死神将他从暴虐凶手掌中彻底解脱。在这场令人痛心的折磨之中，我内心深受罪恶感的强烈谴责，自觉境遇之悲惨远甚世间凡人；我的心灵陷入了难以名状的烦乱骚动；我屡屡渴望一死了之，却又深知自己对那严峻神圣的审判召唤毫无准备。因在营救同胞一事中饱受恶徒构陷欺凌，又为自身灵魂的永恒沉浮忧心如焚，这一切（尤其是后者）将我击打得萎靡至极；致使我视自身为沉重包袱，环顾周遭万物尽是虚空与幻灭，丝毫不能平复饱受煎熬的良知。我再次立意远走土耳其，并决意此生永不再回英格兰。我应聘在开赴土耳其的“韦斯特霍尔号”（Wester Hall，林纳船长 Capt. Linna 统领）充任侍从管事；却因前任船长休斯先生等人从中作梗而化为泡影。一切似乎都在与我为敌，当时我所能得着的唯一宽慰，便是在圣经中读到“日光之下并无新事”，见《传道书》1:9（Eccles. 1:9）；深知凡临到我身的皆是命定，我唯有顺服领受。我便这般满怀沉痛摸索前行，屡屡向上帝发怨言，尤其是对神在冥冥之中的运筹安排心生怨尤；更可怕的是，我甚至开始妄言亵渎，屡屡巴望自己化为任何生灵，哪怕不做人类。在这番剧烈的煎熬激战中，上主以威严可畏的异象警醒我，“人躺在床上打盹，深沉睡卧的时候，神就用梦和夜间的异象”，见《约伯记》33:15（Job 33:15）。神蒙大怜悯，使我看见并在一定程度上领悟了大而可畏的审判之日，即“凡是有淫乱的、沉沦污秽的，在基督和神的国里都是无份的”，见《以弗所书》5:5（Eph. 5:5）。我当时若有可能，宁可将自己的身躯化为世间最卑微的蠕虫；甚至巴不得向群山峻岭呼喊“倒在我们身上吧”，见《启示录》6:16（Rev. 6:16）；然而一切尽属徒劳。我遂恳求神圣的造物主，赐我一小段光阴悔改己身的愚行与深重罪愆，我深知这些罪孽深重难当。上主本其丰盛怜悯，恩准了我的所求，醒来之时见自己仍在阳世之间，感念神之浩瀚洪恩，我四肢百骸虚脱无力长达数分钟之久，浑身瘫软羸弱不堪。

===Original===

This was the first spiritual mercy I ever was sensible of, and being on praying ground, as soon as I recovered a little strength, and got out of bed and dressed myself, I invoked Heaven from my inmost soul, and fervently begged that God would never again permit me to blaspheme his most holy name. The Lord, who is long-suffering, and full of compassion to such poor rebels as we are, condescended to hear and answer. I felt that I was altogether unholy, and saw clearly what a bad use I had made of the faculties I was endowed with; they were given me to glorify God with; I thought, therefore, I had better want them here, and enter into life eternal, than abuse them and be cast into hell fire. I prayed to be directed, if there were any holier than those with whom I was acquainted, that the Lord would point them out to me. I appealed to the Searcher of hearts, whether I did not wish to love him more, and serve him better. Notwithstanding all this, the reader may easily discern, if he is a believer, that I was still in nature’s darkness. At length I hated the house in which I lodged, because God’s most holy name was blasphemed in it; then I saw the word of God verified, viz. “Before they call, I will answer; and while they are yet speaking, I will hear.”
I had a great desire to read the bible the whole day at home; but not having a convenient place for retirement, I left the house in the day, rather than stay amongst the wicked ones; and that day as I was walking, it pleased God to direct me to a house where there was an old seafaring man, who experienced much of the love of God shed abroad in his heart. He began to discourse with me; and, as I desired to love the Lord, his conversation rejoiced me greatly; and indeed I had never heard before the love of Christ to believers set forth in such a manner, and in so clear a point of view. Here I had more questions to put to the man than his time would permit him to answer; and in that memorable hour there came in a dissenting minister; he joined our discourse, and asked me some few questions; among others, where I heard the gospel preached. I knew not what he meant by hearing the gospel; I told him I had read the gospel: and he asked where I went to church, or whether I went at all or not. To which I replied, “I attended St. James’s, St. Martin’s, and St. Ann’s, Soho;”﻿—“So,” said he, “you are a churchman.”

===Chinese===

这是我生平第一次真切体认到的属灵怜悯，既然重履祈祷之地，我稍稍恢复一丝力气，便挣扎下床穿戴整齐，自灵魂深处呼求上苍，虔诚恳求上帝永莫再容许我亵渎其至圣之名。上主常存恒久忍耐，对我们这等卑微悖逆之人充满怜恤，俯允垂听了我的祷告。我深切体会到自己全然污秽不洁，清晰看到自己将神所赐的才赋滥用至何等地步；这些才智本是为了荣耀神而赐；我想自己宁可在阳世间彻底丧失这些才赋而进入永生，也绝不愿滥用才智而被打入地狱永火之中。我祈求神明引领，若世间存有比我所结识者更为圣洁之人，祈求上主为我指引明途。我向上帝鉴察人心之主剖白心迹，叩问自己是否由衷渴盼更爱他、更尽心事奉他。然而尽管如此，读者若是一名真信徒，便能轻易看出我当时依旧深陷自然本性的幽暗之中。最终我对我所寄宿的屋舍心生厌弃，因上帝的至圣之名在屋中屡遭亵渎；此时我亲见神的圣言得以应验，即“他们尚未求告，我就应允；正说话的时候，我就垂听”。

我极度渴望终日在寓所闭门研读圣经；然因缺乏清净避世之所，我宁可日间外出游荡，也不愿与恶人为伍；当日漫步途中，神眷顾引领我步入一间居所，房主是一位饱经风霜的老水手，其心灵深处充盈着沛然倾注的神之慈爱。他开始与我倾心长谈；因我一心渴慕敬爱上主，他的言谈令我欢欣雀跃；坦白而言，我生平从未听人以如此生动清晰的方式阐明基督对信徒的慈爱。在此我向老人提出数不清的困惑，甚至超出了他的时间所能解答的范畴；就在那难忘的时刻，一位独立教派牧师推门而入；他参与了我们的交谈，询问了我几个问题；其中问及我在何处听闻福音宣讲。我全然不知听闻福音究竟所指何意；我答言自己曾阅读福音：他又问我在何处聚会去哪座教堂，抑或根本不去教堂。我答道：“我常去圣詹姆斯堂、圣马丁堂以及索霍的圣安妮堂（St. James's, St. Martin's, and St. Ann's, Soho）；”——“如此说来，”他言道，“你是一位英国国教信徒（churchman）。”

===Original===

I answered, I was. He then invited me to a love-feast at his chapel that evening. I accepted the offer, and thanked him; and soon after he went away, I had some further discourse with the old Christian, added to some profitable reading, which made me exceedingly happy. When I left him he reminded me of coming to the feast; I assured him I would be there. Thus we parted, and I weighed over the heavenly conversation that had passed between these two men, which cheered my then heavy and drooping spirit more than anything I had met with for many months. However, I thought the time long in going to my supposed banquet. I also wished much for the company of these friendly men; their company pleased me much; and I thought the gentlemen very kind, in asking me, a stranger, to a feast; but how singular did it appear to me, to have it in a chapel! When the wished-for hour came I went, and happily the old man was there, who kindly seated me, as he belonged to the place. I was much astonished to see the place filled with people, and no signs of eating and drinking. There were many ministers in the company. At last they began by giving out hymns, and between the singing the minister engaged in prayer; in short, I knew not what to make of this sight, having never seen anything of the kind in my life before now. Some of the guests began to speak their experience, agreeable to what I read in the Scriptures; much was said by every speaker of the providence of God, and his unspeakable mercies, to each of them. This I knew in a great measure, and could most heartily join them. But when they spoke of a future state, they seemed to be altogether certain of their calling and election of God; and that no one could ever separate them from the love of Christ, or pluck them out of his hands. This filled me with utter consternation, intermingled with admiration. I was so amazed as not to know what to think of the company; my heart was attracted and my affections were enlarged. I wished to be as happy as them, and was persuaded in my mind that they were different from the world “that lieth in wickedness,” 1 John 5:19. Their language and singing, etc. did well harmonize; I was entirely overcome, and wished to live and die thus.

===Chinese===

我回答确实如此。他随即热情邀请我参加当晚在他所属礼拜堂举行的爱筵（love-feast）。我欣然应允并向他致谢；他离去后不久，我又与那位年迈基督徒深谈良久，并共同研读了多篇大有裨益的篇章，令我满心喜乐。告辞之际他再次叮嘱我务必赴筵；我郑重保证定如期而至。我们依依惜别，我细细回味这两位长者之间充满属天智慧的交谈，这番甘霖抚慰了我沉重消沉的心灵，其功效远胜数月以来我所经历的任何事物。然而，前往这所自忖之盛宴前，我只觉度时如年。我极其渴望与这群良善之人为伴；他们的同在令我由衷喜悦；这位绅士竟肯邀请我这异乡生客赴宴，实显仁厚至极；然而将宴席设在礼拜堂之中，在我看来又是何等奇特！企盼的时刻终告来临，我依约赴会，幸喜那位老人已在堂中，因属于该堂会友，他亲切地为我安排席位。目睹堂内座无虚席却毫无酒肉饮食之迹象，我大感惊诧。在座会众之中有甚多牧者。他们最终以齐唱赞美诗启幕，在歌声间歇，牧师引领先行祷告；简而言之，我生平从未见过此等场面，全然不知该如何领会眼前景象。部分来宾开始依圣经所记分享自身的蒙恩见证；每位讲员皆深情倾诉了神的奇妙看顾，以及临到各人身上的莫大慈爱。这一切我亦深有共鸣，能够由衷与之契合。然而当他们谈及将来的永恒境地时，每个人似乎对神对他们的选召与拣选深信不疑；笃信永无任何人能将他们与基督的爱隔绝，或将他们从基督圣手中夺去。这令我惊愕万状，心中交织着难以言表的敬仰。我惊叹得不知该如何看待这群会众；我的心被深深吸引，情感为之激荡拓宽。我渴盼能如他们一般幸福喜乐，深信他们确乎不同于那“卧在恶者手下”的世俗世界，见《约翰一书》5:19（1 John 5:19）。他们的言辞与歌咏和谐相融；我被彻底征服，但愿能在此等境界中同生共死。

===Original===

Lastly, some persons in the place produced some neat baskets full of buns, which they distributed about; and each person communicated with his neighbour, and sipped water out of different mugs, which they handed about to all who were present. This kind of Christian fellowship I had never seen, nor ever thought of seeing on earth; it fully reminded me of what I had read in the holy scriptures, of the primitive Christians, who loved each other and broke bread. In partaking of it, even from house to house, this entertainment (which lasted about four hours) ended in singing and prayer. It was the first soul feast I ever was present at. This last twenty-four hours produced me things, spiritual and temporal, sleeping and waking, judgment and mercy, that I could not but admire the goodness of God, in directing the blind, blasphemous sinner in the path that he knew not of, even among the just; and instead of judgment he has showed mercy, and will hear and answer the prayers and supplications of every returning prodigal:

> O! to grace how great a debtor

Daily I’m constrain’d to be!

===Chinese===

最终，堂内有人捧出数只盛满小圆面包（buns）的精致柳条篮，逐一分发众人；各人彼此分享相交，并传递各色水杯互啜清水，递遍在场全员。此等基督徒团契相交之景我平生未见，亦未曾料想能在尘世得见；这全然唤醒了我昔日在圣经中所读初代信徒彼此相爱、同心擘饼之记载。甚至逐家逐户同领此宴，这场相聚（持续约 4 个小时）在歌咏与祈祷中圆满礼成。这是我平生出席的第一场灵魂盛宴。这过去的 24 小时，向我显明了属灵与属世、沉睡与苏醒、审判与怜悯之种种奇迹，使我不得不由衷赞叹上帝的至善，他引领我这瞎眼盲目、妄发亵渎之罪人，行在素所不识的光明坦途之上，甚至置身义人行列之中；他以怜悯代替审判，必垂听并应允每一个浪子回头的祈求与哀告：

> 浩瀚恩典何深重，
> 日日促我作负债人！

===Original===

After this I was resolved to win Heaven if possible; and if I perished I thought it should be at the feet of Jesus, in praying to him for salvation. After having been an eyewitness to some of the happiness which attended those who feared God, I knew not how, with any propriety, to return to my lodgings, where the name of God was continually profaned, at which I felt the greatest horror. I paused in my mind for some time, not knowing what to do; whether to hire a bed elsewhere, or go home again. At last, fearing an evil report might arise, I went home, with a farewell to card-playing and vain jesting, etc. I saw that time was very short, eternity long, and very near, and I viewed those persons alone blessed who were found ready at midnight call, or when the Judge of all, both quick and dead, cometh.
The next day I took courage, and went to Holborn, to see my new and worthy acquaintance, the old man, Mr. C﻿—﻿—; he, with his wife, a gracious woman, were at work at silk weaving; they seemed mutually happy, and both quite glad to see me, and I more so to see them. I sat down, and we conversed much about soul matters, etc. Their discourse was amazingly delightful, edifying, and pleasant. I knew not at last how to leave this agreeable pair, till time summoned me away. As I was going they lent me a little book, entitled *The Conversion of an Indian*. It was in questions and answers. The poor man came over the sea to London, to inquire after the Christian’s God, who, (through rich mercy) he found, and had not his journey in vain. The above book was of great use to me, and at that time was a means of strengthening my faith; however, in parting, they both invited me to call on them when I pleased. This delighted me, and I took care to make all the improvement from it I could; and so far I thanked God for such company and desires. I prayed that the many evils I felt within might be done away, and that I might be weaned from my former carnal acquaintances. This was quickly heard and answered, and I was soon connected with those whom the scripture calls the excellent of the earth. I heard the gospel preached, and the thoughts of my heart and actions were laid open by the preachers, and the way of salvation by Christ alone was evidently set forth.

===Chinese===

自此之后，我决意竭尽全力博取天国；纵然粉身碎骨，我也愿仆倒在耶稣脚前，向他叩求救赎之恩。亲眼目睹了敬畏神之人所享有的幸福荣美之后，我实在不知该如何端庄体面地重返那座亵渎上帝圣名之声不绝于耳的寓所，对此我深感惊恐战栗。我内心沉吟良久，不知如何抉择；究竟该另择他处租宿床位，抑或重返寓所。最终因担忧流言蜚语，我还是回到了寓所，并彻底诀别了牌戏与虚妄笑谈等世俗恶习。我深悟现世苦短，永恒既长且近，深知唯有在午夜呼召来临、或审判活人死人之主降临之际时刻警醒预备周全之人，方为真有福之人。

次日我鼓足勇气前往霍尔本（Holborn），拜会我的新知己那位德高望重的老人 C——先生；他与贤德温良的妻子正在织机前纺织丝绸；夫妇二人和睦融洽，见我登门欣喜异常，我见彼等更添欢喜。我安坐下来，彼此就灵魂归宿等属灵要务展开深谈。他们的交谈令人无比陶醉，既大得造就又心旷神怡。我深陷于这怡人夫妇的雅叙之中难舍难分，直至时限催促我起身告辞。离去之际，他们借予我一本题为《一个印第安人的归信》（The Conversion of an Indian）的问答体小册子。讲述一个可怜的印第安人漂洋过海来到伦敦，寻求基督徒之上帝，并（蒙丰盛怜悯）终得寻见，未曾虚度此行。此书对我大有裨益，在当时成了坚固我信心的良器；告别时他们热情邀我随时造访。这令我欢欣鼓舞，我竭力汲取其中的属灵益处；为此等良伴与渴慕由衷感戴天恩。我祈求拔除内心郁积的诸多恶念，使我彻底断绝与往昔属肉体旧相识的往来。这祷告迅速蒙垂听应允，未几我便结识了圣经中被称为世上尊贵圣洁之人。我亲耳聆听福音宣讲，心中的隐秘意念与平生行径被讲员层层剖析展现无遗，唯独靠基督得救之道亦被清晰明了地陈明于眼前。

===Original===

Thus I went on happily for near two months; and I once heard, during this period, a reverend gentleman speak of a man who had departed this life in full assurance of his going to glory. I was much astonished at the assertion; and did very deliberately inquire how he could get at this knowledge. I was answered fully, agreeable to what I read in the oracles of truth; and was told also, that if I did not experience the new birth, and the pardon of my sins, through the blood of Christ, before I died, I could not enter the kingdom of heaven. I knew not what to think of this report, as I thought I kept eight commandments out of ten; then my worthy interpreter told me I did not do it, nor could I; and he added, that no man ever did or could keep the commandments, without offending in one point. I thought this sounded very strange, and puzzled me much for many weeks; for I thought it a hard saying. I then asked my friend, Mr. L﻿—﻿—﻿d, who was a clerk in a chapel, why the commandments of God were given, if we could not be saved by them? To which he replied, “The law is a schoolmaster to bring us to Christ,” who alone could and did keep the commandments, and fulfilled all their requirements for his elect people, even those to whom he had given a living faith, and the sins of those chosen vessels *were already* atoned for and forgiven them whilst living; and if I did not experience the same before my exit, the Lord would say at that great day to me “Go ye cursed,” etc. etc. for God would appear faithful in his judgments to the wicked, as he would be faithful in showing mercy to those who were ordained to it before the world was; therefore Christ Jesus seemed to be all in all to that man’s soul. I was much wounded at this discourse, and brought into such a dilemma as I never expected. I asked him, if *he* was to die that moment, whether he was sure to enter the kingdom of God? and added, “Do you *know* that your sins are forgiven you?” He answered in the affirmative. Then confusion, anger, and discontent seized me, and I staggered much at this sort of doctrine; it brought me to a stand, not knowing which to believe, whether salvation by works or by faith only in Christ. I requested him to tell me how I might know when my sins were forgiven me. He assured me he could not, and that none but God alone could do this.

===Chinese===

我就这样满怀喜乐度过了将近 2 个月的光阴；在此期间，我曾听一位令人肃然起敬的牧者提及一人，在离世之际对进入荣耀天国满怀确信。我对这一断言惊诧不已；遂审慎细致地探问他何以能获知此等确信。我的疑问得到了详尽解答，全然契合真理启示之训导；更有人告诫我，若在离世之前未曾经历重生，未曾借着基督的宝血蒙赦免罪愆，我便绝不能进入天国。面对此言我不知如何是好，自忖十诫之中我守全了八条；然而那位德高望重的解惑长者却直言我并未守全，亦根本无力守全；他进一步指出，世人若非在一处跌足，断无人能守全全部诫命。我觉得此言极其离奇，困扰了我数周之久；在我看来这实在是一句难以领受的硬话。我遂叩问在礼拜堂充任堂差的挚友 L——d 先生：若我们无法靠诫命得救，上帝又何以赐下诫命？他答道：“律法是我们引向基督的启蒙师傅，”唯有基督能够且确实守全了诫命，并为其选民成全了诫命的一切规条，即赐予活泼信心的子民，这些蒙选器皿的罪愆在生前便已被赎买且蒙完全赦免；我若在离世前未能体验此等救赎，到了审判大日主必斥我道“你们这些被咒诅的人，离开我吧”云云；因为上帝在审判恶人上显为公义信实，正如他在向创世前预定蒙恩之人施怜悯上显为信实一样；因之基督耶稣在彼人心中俨然是一切的一切。这番训导令我深受刺痛，将我推入前所未有的绝境。我问他，假若他本人在此时此刻猝然离世，他是否确信自己能进入天国？并追问：“你确知自己的罪已蒙赦免了吗？”他给予了肯定的答复。闻听此言，惶惑、愤懑与不满齐齐涌上心头，我对这等教义剧烈动摇；它令我陷入停滞徘徊，不知究竟当信靠行为称义，抑或单凭仰赖基督的信心得救。我恳求他指点我如何方能确知自身罪愆已蒙赦免。他坦言自己无能为力，唯有上帝亲自启示方能成全。

===Original===

I told him it was very mysterious; but he said it was really matter of fact, and quoted many portions of scripture immediately to the point, to which I could make no reply. He then desired me to pray to God to show me these things. I answered, that I prayed to God every day. He said, “I perceive you are a churchman.” I answered I was. He then entreated me to beg of God to show me what I was, and the true state of my soul. I thought the prayer very short and odd; so we parted for that time. I weighed all these things well over, and could not help thinking how it was possible for a man to know that his sins were forgiven him in this life. I wished that God would reveal this self same thing unto me. In a short time after this I went to Westminster chapel; the Rev. Mr. P﻿—﻿— preached, from Lam. 3:39. It was a wonderful sermon; he clearly showed that a living man had no cause to complain for the punishment of his sins; he evidently justified the Lord in all his dealings with the sons of men; he also showed the justice of God in the eternal punishment of the wicked and impenitent. The discourse seemed to me like a two-edged sword cutting all ways; it afforded me much joy, intermingled with many fears, about my soul; and when it was ended, he gave it out that he intended, the ensuing week, to examine all those who meant to attend the Lord’s table. Now I thought much of my good works, and at the same time was doubtful of my being a proper object to receive the sacrament; I was full of meditation till the day of examining. However, I went to the chapel, and, though much distressed, I addressed the reverend gentleman, thinking, if I was not right, he would endeavour to convince me of it. When I conversed with him, the first thing he asked me was, what I knew of Christ? I told him I believed in him, and had been baptized in his name. “Then,” said he, “when were you brought to the knowledge of God? and how were you convinced of sin?” I knew not what he meant by these questions; I told him I kept eight commandments out of ten; but that I sometimes swore on board ship, and sometimes when on shore, and broke the sabbath. He then asked me if I could read? I answered, “Yes.”﻿—“Then,” said he, “do you not read in the bible, he that offends in one point is guilty of all?” I said, “Yes.”

===Chinese===

我言此事实在神妙莫测；然而他言此乃千真万确之铁证，并当场引经据典，列举多处圣经章节直指要害，令我哑口无言。他遂叮嘱我向上帝切切祈求，祈求主将这些奥秘显明与我。我答称自己每日皆向上帝祈祷。他言道：“我看出你是一位英国国教徒。”我答称正是。他遂恳请我求告上帝，向我显明我本相的虚妄以及我灵魂的真实境况。我觉得这祈祷辞极其简短古怪；于是我们暂时作别。我将这些要务在心头反复权衡，不禁深思人在今世究竟何以能确知自身罪孽已蒙赦免。我热切期盼上帝能将这同一桩奥秘亲自启示于我。此后不久我前往威斯敏斯特礼拜堂（Westminster chapel）；P——牧师（Rev. Mr. P------）正在开坛讲道，经文取自《耶利米哀歌》3:39（Lam. 3:39）。那是一场震撼人心的宣讲；他精辟透彻地阐明，活人因己身的罪受罚，绝无理由发怨言；他雄辩地昭示了上主在对待世人的一切作为上尽皆公义；更揭示了上帝在对顽梗不化之恶人施以永恒刑罚上彰显的至高公义。这篇讲道于我犹如双刃利剑四面挥斩；它带给我无尽的欣喜，却也交织着对自身灵魂永恒沉沦的无边惊惧；讲道落幕之际，牧师宣告计划于翌周考察考问所有意欲领受圣餐之人。此时我满脑子皆是自己的善行义举，同时又深切怀疑自己是否配得领受圣餐；直至考核之日，我皆深陷在沉思苦省之中。无论如何我还是来到了礼拜堂，虽满心愁苦，依然上前拜见这位尊敬的牧师，心忖若自己有所偏差，他定会尽力开导于我。交谈伊始，他劈头问我对基督有何认识？我答称我相信基督，并在他圣名之下受了洗。“那么，”他问，“你何时被引领归向真神的？又是如何省察领悟自身罪恶的？”面对这些问话我不知所云；我答称十诫中我守全了八条；只是有时在船上偶发咒骂，有时在岸上亦难免破戒，且偶有干犯安息日之过。他随即问我是否识字？我答：“识得。”——“那么，”他问，“难道你未曾在圣经中读到，凡在一条上跌倒的，便是犯了众条吗？”我答：“读过。”

===Original===

Then he assured me, that one sin unatoned for was as sufficient to damn a soul as one leak was to sink a ship. Here I was struck with awe; for the minister exhorted me much, and reminded me of the shortness of time, and the length of eternity, and that no unregenerate soul, or anything unclean, could enter the kingdom of Heaven. He did not admit me as a communicant; but recommended me to read the scriptures, and hear the word preached, not to neglect fervent prayer to God, who has promised to hear the supplications of those who seek him in godly sincerity; so I took my leave of him, with many thanks, and resolved to follow his advice, so far as the Lord would condescend to enable me. During this time I was out of employ, nor was I likely to get a situation suitable for me, which obliged me to go once more to sea. I engaged as steward of a ship called the *Hope*, Capt. Richard Strange, bound from London to Cadiz in Spain. In a short time after I was on board I heard the name of God much blasphemed, and I feared greatly, lest I should catch the horrible infection. I thought if I sinned again, after having life and death set evidently before me, I should certainly go to hell. My mind was uncommonly chagrined, and I murmured much at God’s providential dealings with me, and was discontented with the commandments, that I could not be saved by what I had done; I hated all things, and wished I had never been born; confusion seized me, and I wished to be annihilated. One day I was standing on the very edge of the stern of the ship, thinking to drown myself; but this scripture was instantly impressed on my mind﻿—“that no murderer hath eternal life abiding in him,” 1 John 3:15. Then I paused, and thought myself the unhappiest man living. Again I was convinced that the Lord was better to me than I deserved, and I was better off in the world than many. After this I began to fear death; I fretted, mourned, and prayed, till I became a burden to others, but more so to myself. At length I concluded to beg my bread on shore rather than go again to sea amongst a people who feared not God, and I entreated the captain three different times to discharge me; he would not, but each time gave me greater and greater encouragement to continue with him, and all on board showed me very great civility: notwithstanding all this I was unwilling to embark again.

===Chinese===

他随即郑重告诫我，一项未蒙代赎之罪便足以令灵魂沉沦下地狱，正如一处微小的裂口足以令万石巨舰沉没汪洋。我被深深震慑；因牧师深切劝勉于我，警醒我光阴之短促、永恒之深远，以及凡未得重生之灵魂与不洁之物，绝不能踏入天国半步。他并未接纳我为领餐会友；却极力勉励我殷勤研读圣经、多听真道宣讲，切莫懈怠向神发出热切祈求，因上帝曾立下圣约，凡以圣洁敬虔之诚心寻求他者，必蒙垂听回应；我遂满怀感激向他作别，立誓在主俯允赐力的限度内，恪守他的劝导。在此期间我赋闲无业，亦难以寻得合宜之差事，被迫再度涉海谋生。我应聘在一艘名为“希望号”（Hope）的商船上充任管事侍从，该船由理查德·斯特兰奇船长（Capt. Richard Strange）统领，由伦敦开赴西班牙加的斯（Cadiz in Spain）。登船未久，我便时常耳闻亵渎上帝圣名之秽言，我惊惧万状，深恐染上这可怕的瘟疫恶疾。我想自己若在生死祸福已昭然陈明眼前之后再度犯罪，必径直坠入地狱无疑。我内心沮丧愤懑至极，对神加诸我身的冥冥安排屡发怨言，更对诫命心怀不满，愤懑于单凭己身作为竟无法博得拯救；我厌弃尘世万物，甚至怨恨自己为何降生人世；神思错乱将我彻底吞噬，我竟巴望自己化为齑粉虚无。某日我伫立于船尾边缘，企图投海自尽；然而经上的圣言瞬间如洪钟大吕撞击我的心扉——“凡恨弟兄的，就是杀人的；你们知道凡杀人的，没有永生存在他里面”，见《约翰一书》3:15（1 John 3:15）。我戛然而止，自忖系世间最凄惨不幸之人。转念间我深知上主待我远胜于我所配得，在世间的际遇亦胜过许多受苦之人。自此我开始畏惧死亡；我烦躁、哀伤、苦祷，终至成了他人的沉重累赘，对自己更是难以承受之重负。最终我打定主意宁可在陆上乞讨为生，也不愿再随一群不敬畏上帝之徒泛海沧波，我先后 3 次苦求船长准予我离职解聘；他皆坚辞不允，反而每次都愈发诚挚勉励我留船协助，全船船员亦待我极其客气尊崇：尽管如此，我依然万分不愿再度扬帆远航。

===Original===

At last some of my religious friends advised me, by saying it was my lawful calling, consequently it was my duty to obey, and that God was not confined to place, etc. etc. particularly Mr. G. S. the governor of Tothil-fields Bridewell, who pitied my case, and read the eleventh chapter of the Hebrews to me, with exhortations. He prayed for me, and I believed that he prevailed on my behalf, as my burden was then greatly removed, and I found a heartfelt resignation to the will of God. The good man gave me a pocket Bible and *Allen’s Alarm* to the unconverted. We parted, and the next day I went on board again. We sailed for Spain, and I found favour with the captain. It was the fourth of the month of September when we sailed from London; we had a delightful voyage to Cadiz, where we arrived the twenty-third of the same month. The place is strong, commands a fine prospect, and is very rich. The Spanish galleons frequent that port, and some arrived whilst we were there. I had many opportunities of reading the scriptures. I wrestled hard with God in fervent prayer, who had declared in his word that he would hear the groanings and deep sighs of the poor in spirit. I found this verified to my utter astonishment and comfort in the following manner:
On the morning of the 6th of October, (I pray you to attend) or all that day, I thought that I should either see or hear something supernatural. I had a secret impulse on my mind of something that was to take place, which drove me continually for that time to a throne of grace. It pleased God to enable me to wrestle with him, as Jacob did: I prayed that if sudden death were to happen, and I perished, it might be at Christ’s feet.

===Chinese===

最终，几位虔敬的信友劝导我，言此乃我正当安身立命之合法职业，遵从乃是我的本分，且神的存在绝不受地域阻隔等等；尤其是托特希尔菲尔兹教养所（Tothil-fields Bridewell）所长 G. S. 先生，极度怜悯我的境遇，为我诵读了《希伯来书》第 11 章，并谆谆劝勉。他为我献上切切祈求，我深信他的代祷在上帝面前大有功效，因我心头的沉重枷锁随即大大消解，内心涌起对上帝旨意的由衷顺服。这位良善长者赠我一本袖珍圣经与一册《艾伦警钟》（Allen’s Alarm to the unconverted）。我们依依惜别，次日我重新登船就职。我们扬帆开赴西班牙，我深得船长厚爱眷顾。自伦敦启航之日乃是 9 月 4 日；航程顺遂惬意直抵加的斯，我们于同月 23 日平安抵埠。加的斯壁垒森严，景致恢宏秀美，极其富庶繁华。西班牙运宝大帆船（galleons）常年出入该港，我们逗留期间便有数艘进港。我有甚多闲暇研读圣经。我以极度热切之祷告与神竭力较力，因神曾在圣言中庄严宣告，他必垂听困苦虚心之人的呻吟与深沉叹息。这一应许在随后以令我万分惊异与欣慰的方式得到了完全的应验：

在 10 月 6 日清晨（祈请读者万分留心），抑或整整一日之中，我都预感到自己必将亲见或亲耳聆听某种超自然的奇迹。我内心深处萌生出一股神秘的预感，知晓必有大事发生，这驱使我整日不停地奔向施恩宝座。蒙神开恩，使我能如雅各当年那般与神彻夜较力：我恳切祈求，若骤然暴毙大限降临而我不得不死，但愿能仆倒在基督的脚前。

===Original===

In the evening of the same day, as I was reading and meditating on the fourth chapter of the Acts, twelfth verse, under the solemn apprehensions of eternity, and reflecting on my past actions, I began to think I had lived a moral life, and that I had a proper ground to believe I had an interest in the divine favour; but still meditating on the subject, not knowing whether salvation was to be had partly for our own good deeds, or solely as the sovereign gift of God; in this deep consternation the Lord was pleased to break in upon my soul with his bright beams of heavenly light; and in an instant as it were, removing the veil, and letting light into a dark place, I saw clearly with the eye of faith the crucified Saviour bleeding on the cross on mount Calvary: the scriptures became an unsealed book, I saw myself a condemned criminal under the law, which came with its full force to my conscience, and when “the commandment came sin revived, and I died,” I saw the Lord Jesus Christ in his humiliation, loaded and bearing my reproach, sin, and shame. I then clearly perceived that by the deeds of the law no flesh living could be justified. I was then convinced that by the first Adam sin came, and by the second Adam (the Lord Jesus Christ) all that are saved must be made alive. It was given me at that time to know what it was to be born again, John 3:5. I saw the eighth chapter to the Romans, and the doctrines of God’s decrees, verified agreeable to his eternal, everlasting, and unchangeable purposes. The word of God was sweet to my taste, yea sweeter than honey and the honeycomb. Christ was revealed to my soul as the chiefest among ten thousand. These heavenly moments were really as life to the dead, and what John calls an earnest of the Spirit.22 This was indeed unspeakable, and I firmly believe undeniable by many. Now every leading providential circumstance that happened to me, from the day I was taken from my parents to that hour, was then in my view, as if it had but just then occurred. I was sensible of the invisible hand of God, which guided and protected me when in truth I knew it not: still the Lord pursued me although I slighted and disregarded it; this mercy melted me down. When I considered my poor wretched state I wept, seeing what a great debtor I was to sovereign free grace.

===Chinese===

同日傍晚，当我在永恒的庄严敬畏中，研读并默想《使徒行传》第 4 章第 12 节，反躬自省平生作为之时，我一度以为自己平生恪守道德生活，理应有充分凭据相信自己享有神之恩宠；然反复深思此道，仍莫知救恩究竟部分系因自身善行所致，抑或全然是上帝主权的至高赏赐；置身此等深沉震撼之中，上主蒙大恩典，以属天光明的璀璨光芒猛然穿透破入我的灵魂；宛若揭开面纱，将光明洒入幽暗之地，我以信心的双眼，赫然清晰目睹被钉十字架的救主在各各他山（mount Calvary）上流血受难：圣经封印豁然解开，我赫然照见自己在律法之下乃是一个罪无可赦的死囚，律法携万钧雷霆重压于我的良知之上，“诫命来到，罪又活了，我就死了”；我亲眼目睹主耶稣基督在降卑受辱之中，背负担当了我所有的羞辱、罪恶与过犯。我方始彻底看清，凡有血气的没有一个能因行律法在神面前称义。我深信罪恶由前亚当而来，唯有借着末后的亚当（即主耶稣基督），一切得救之人才得以重获生命。当时上主恩赐于我，使我真正领悟了何谓重生，见《约翰福音》3:5（John 3:5）。我真切看清了《罗马书》第 8 章以及上帝预定恩旨之教义，全然印证了上帝永恒不变之圣意。神的圣言于我甘甜无比，胜过蜂蜜，比下滴之蜂房更甜。基督在我灵魂中彰显为万人中之至尊者。这些属天的神圣时刻于我真若枯骨复生，亦正是约翰所言之圣灵的凭据，见附注 22。此等体验实非笔墨所能尽述，我深信亦绝非世人所能抹杀。此时自我脱离双亲之日起直至那一刻，平生所历之一切幽微神意，皆如历历在目般重陈眼前。我真切感知到那只无形的神圣巨手，在昔日我全然不知之中一路引领庇佑着我：纵我肆意怠慢背弃，上主依旧不离不弃一路追寻召回；此等浩荡洪恩令我彻底消融化解。思想自己昔日何等卑微可怜之惨状，我痛哭流涕，深感自己面对上帝至高白白的浩荡恩典，欠下了何等深重的恩债。

===Original===

Now the Ethiopian was willing to be saved by Jesus Christ, the sinner’s only surety, and also to rely on none other person or thing for salvation. Self was obnoxious, and good works he had none, for it is God that worketh in us both to will and to do. The amazing things of that hour can never be told﻿—it was joy in the Holy Ghost! I felt an astonishing change; the burden of sin, the gaping jaws of hell, and the fears of death, that weighed me down before, now lost their horror; indeed I thought death would now be the best earthly friend I ever had. Such were my grief and joy as I believe are seldom experienced. I was bathed in tears, and said, What am I that God should thus look on me the vilest of sinners? I felt a deep concern for my mother and friends, which occasioned me to pray with fresh ardour; and, in the abyss of thought, I viewed the unconverted people of the world in a very awful state, being without God and without hope.
It pleased God to pour out on me the Spirit of prayer and the grace of supplication, so that in loud acclamations I was enabled to praise and glorify his most holy name. When I got out of the cabin, and told some of the people what the Lord had done for me, alas, who could understand me or believe my report!﻿—None but to whom the arm of the Lord was revealed. I became a barbarian to them in talking of the love of Christ: his name was to me as ointment poured forth; indeed it was sweet to my soul, but to them a rock of offence. I thought my case singular, and every hour a day until I came to London, for I much longed to be with some to whom I could tell of the wonders of God’s love towards me, and join in prayer to him whom my soul loved and thirsted after. I had uncommon commotions within, such as few can tell aught about. Now the bible was my only companion and comfort; I prized it much, with many thanks to God that I could read it for myself, and was not left to be tossed about or led by man’s devices and notions. The worth of a soul cannot be told.﻿—May the Lord give the reader an understanding in this. Whenever I looked in the bible I saw things new, and many texts were immediately applied to me with great comfort, for I knew that to me was the word of salvation sent.

===Chinese===

如今这个埃塞俄比亚人（Ethiopian）终于甘心情愿完全接受由耶稣基督这位罪人唯一的保局者施行救赎，不再倚靠世间任何凡人或外物求取自救。自我变得可憎可弃，自身善行荡然无存，因为立志行事尽皆是上帝在我们心里运行成全。那一时刻所显现之奇迹非凡人所能尽述——那正是圣灵中的狂喜！我体会到了一种脱胎换骨的奇妙巨变；昔日重重压迫我的罪恶重负、地狱张开的血盆巨口以及对死亡的无边恐惧，此刻皆尽失其威；甚至于我而言，死亡已化为我在世间所能拥有的最至善之友朋。这番交织的悲戚与狂喜，深信世间罕有人曾体味。我泪流满面，呼喊道：我算什么，上帝竟肯如此顾念垂怜我这罪人中的罪魁？我对家慈与亲朋萌生出刻骨铭心的牵挂，促使我以全新的热忱为他们切切代祷；在沉思的幽谷中，我深感世上未得救之人陷入了何等可怖的绝境，他们处于无神无望的茫茫长夜之中。

蒙神恩典，将祷告的灵与恳求的恩沛然浇灌于我，令我得以放声赞美荣耀其至圣之名。当我跨出船舱，向船员倾吐上主在我身上所行的奇事时，呜呼，世间又有谁能领会我的倾诉或相信我的见证！——唯有那些耶和华的臂膀向其显露之人方能领悟。我在他们眼中谈论基督之爱浑如蛮夷狂语：他的圣名于我如倾倒的香膏，于我的灵魂甘甜馨香，于他们却是绊脚的顽石。我觉得自己的境遇千载难逢，度日如年渴盼重返伦敦，因我极度渴望置身于同道知己之间，向他们传述上帝临到我身的浩荡恩爱，并与我心所爱慕渴求的救主同心祈祷。我内心涌动着非同寻常的激荡狂澜，其奥秘罕有人能道其一二。如今圣经成了我唯一的良伴与安慰；我视若拱璧，由衷感谢上帝使我能亲眼捧读，未曾沦为任由凡人之诡计私见摆布漂荡之浮萍。灵魂的无价不可估量。——愿上主赐予读者洞察此理之悟性。每当我翻开圣经，总见全新启示，多处经文带着莫大抚慰直扣我的心扉，因我确知这得救之圣道乃是专为我而降。

===Original===

Sure I was that the Spirit which indited the word opened my heart to receive the truth of it as it is in Jesus﻿—that the same Spirit enabled me to act faith upon the promises that were so precious to me, and enabled me to believe to the salvation of my soul. By free grace I was persuaded that I had a part in the first resurrection, and was “enlightened with the light of the living,” Job 33:30. I wished for a man of God with whom I might converse: my soul was like the chariots of Aminidab, Canticles 6:12. These, among others, were the precious promises that were so powerfully applied to me: “All things whatsoever ye shall ask in prayer, believing, ye shall receive,” Mat. 21:22. “Peace I leave with you, my peace I give unto you,” John 14:27. I saw the blessed Redeemer to be the fountain of life, and the well of salvation. I experienced him all in all; he had brought me by a way that I knew not, and he had made crooked paths straight. Then in his name I set up my Ebenezer, saying, Hitherto he hath helped me: and could say to the sinners about me, Behold what a Saviour I have! Thus I was, by the teaching of that all-glorious Deity, the great One in Three, and Three in One, confirmed in the truths of the bible, those oracles of everlasting truth, on which every soul living must stand or fall eternally, agreeable to Acts 4:12. “Neither is there salvation in any other, for there is none other name under heaven given among men whereby we must be saved, but only Christ Jesus.” May God give the reader a right understanding in these facts! To him that believeth all things are possible, but to them that are unbelieving nothing is pure, Titus 1:15. During this period we remained at Cadiz until our ship got laden. We sailed about the fourth of November; and, having a good passage, we arrived in London the month following, to my comfort, with heartfelt gratitude to God for his rich and unspeakable mercies. On my return I had but one text which puzzled me, or that the devil endeavoured to buffet me with, viz. Rom. 6:6. and, as I had heard of the Reverend Mr. Romaine, and his great knowledge in the scriptures, I wished much to hear him preach. One day I went to Blackfriars church, and, to my great satisfaction and surprise, he preached from that very text.

===Chinese===

我深信正是默示圣言的圣灵开启了我的心门，使我领受耶稣基督里所包含的真理——正是同一位圣灵加添我信心，使我能抓住这无比宝贵的应许，笃信不疑以致灵魂得救。借着白白的恩典，我深信自己在头一次的复活里有份，并“得蒙光照，与活人一样享有生命之光”，见《约伯记》33:30（Job 33:30）。我极度渴望能与一位属神之人长谈交心：我的心境正如亚米拿达的战车，见《雅歌》6:12（Canticles 6:12）。在诸多经文中，以下宝贵应许尤为大有能力地印刻在我心头：“你们祷告，无论求什么，只要信，就必得着”，见《马太福音》21:22（Mat. 21:22）；“我留下平安给你们，我将我的平安赐给你们”，见《约翰福音》14:27（John 14:27）。我目睹荣耀的救赎主乃是生命之源泉、救恩之深井。我亲身体验到他是一切的一切；他引领我行在素不相识的道路之上，使弯曲之地化为坦途。我遂奉他的圣名立起以利以谢（Ebenezer）之石，宣告说：到如今耶和华都帮助我；并能向周遭的罪人们高呼：看哪，我有一位何等荣耀的救主！因之，借着至高荣耀的三位一体独一真神的训导，我在圣经真理中得以完全确立坚固，这乃是永恒真理之圣谕，天下凡有血气者皆以此立定或沉沦，全然契合《使徒行传》4:12（Acts 4:12）之宣告：“除他以外，别无拯救；因为在天下人间，没有赐下别的名，我们可以靠着得救，唯有基督耶稣。”愿上帝赐予读者领悟这些真相之真知灼见！在信的人凡事都能，在不信的人没有一样是洁净的，见《提多书》1:15（Titus 1:15）。在此期间我们驻泊加的斯直至装载告竣。我们约于 11 月 4 日扬帆启航；航程顺畅，于次月平安抵达伦敦，令我心深得安慰，由衷感恩上帝赐下的丰盛莫测之洪恩。重返伦敦后，唯有一处经文令我困惑不解，亦即魔鬼借以屡屡折磨攻击我者，那便是《罗马书》6:6（Rom. 6:6）；因我久闻威廉·罗曼牧师（Reverend Mr. Romaine）之大名及其深邃之经学造诣，极盼聆听其宣讲。一日我前往黑修士教堂（Blackfriars church），令我万分欣慰惊奇的是，他所宣讲的经文恰恰正是那一章节！

===Original===

He very clearly showed the difference between human works and free election, which is according to God’s sovereign will and pleasure. These glad tidings set me entirely at liberty, and I went out of the church rejoicing, seeing my spots were those of God’s children. I went to Westminster Chapel, and saw some of my old friends, who were glad when they perceived the wonderful change that the Lord had wrought in me, particularly Mr. G﻿—﻿— S﻿—﻿—, my worthy acquaintance, who was a man of a choice spirit, and had great zeal for the Lord’s service. I enjoyed his correspondence till he died in the year 1784. I was again examined at that same chapel, and was received into church fellowship amongst them: I rejoiced in spirit, making melody in my heart to the God of all my mercies. Now my whole wish was to be dissolved, and to be with Christ﻿—but, alas! I must wait mine appointed time.

===Chinese===

他极其透彻明了地阐明了凡人行为与白白拣选之间的天壤之别，昭示这全然源自上帝至高主权之旨意与恩悦。这福音佳音使我彻底重获自由，我满心欢喜步出教堂，看清自己身上的斑点乃是神之儿女的印记。我前往威斯敏斯特礼拜堂，重逢昔日几位挚友，见上主在我身上所成的奇妙巨变，众人皆欢欣称颂；尤以我德高望重之旧识 G—— S—— 先生为甚，此君怀抱圣洁灵命，对事奉上主满怀热忱。我一直与他书信往来，直至他在 1784 年安息主怀。我在该礼拜堂再次接受考问接纳，正式被接纳进入教会团契相交之中：我在心灵深处喜乐歌咏，向赐我诸般怜悯之上帝奏响心灵的和乐。如今我毕生唯一的企盼，便是情愿离世归天与基督同在——然而呜呼！我仍须忍耐等候神为我所命定之时刻。

## XI / 第十一章
===Original===

The author embarks on board a ship bound for Cadiz﻿—Is near being shipwrecked﻿—Goes to Malaga﻿—Remarkable fine cathedral there﻿—The author disputes with a popish priest﻿—Picking up eleven miserable men at sea in returning to England﻿—Engages again with Doctor Irving to accompany him to Jamaica and the Mosquito Shore﻿—Meets with an Indian prince on board﻿—The author attempts to instruct him in the truths of the Gospel﻿—Frustrated by the bad example of some in the ship﻿—They arrive on the Mosquito Shore with some slaves they purchased at Jamaica, and begin to cultivate a plantation﻿—Some account of the manners and customs of the Mosquito Indians﻿—Successful device of the author’s to quell a riot among them﻿—Curious entertainment given by them to Doctor Irving and the author, who leaves the shore and goes for Jamaica﻿—Is barbarously treated by a man with whom he engaged for his passage﻿—Escapes and goes to the Mosquito admiral, who treats him kindly﻿—He gets another vessel and goes on board﻿—Instances of bad treatment﻿—Meets Doctor Irving﻿—Gets to Jamaica﻿—Is cheated by his captain﻿—Leaves the Doctor and goes for England.
When our ship was got ready for sea again, I was entreated by the captain to go in her once more; but, as I felt myself now as happy as I could wish to be in this life, I for some time refused; however, the advice of my friends at last prevailed; and, in full resignation to the will of God, I again embarked for Cadiz in March 1775. We had a very good passage, without any material accident, until we arrived off the Bay of Cadiz; when one Sunday, just as we were going into the harbour, the ship struck against a rock and knocked off a garboard plank, which is the next to the keel. In an instant all hands were in the greatest confusion, and began with loud cries to call on God to have mercy on them. Although I could not swim, and saw no way of escaping death, I felt no dread in my then situation, having no desire to live. I even rejoiced in spirit, thinking this death would be sudden glory. But the fullness of time was not yet come. The people near to me were much astonished in seeing me thus calm and resigned; but I told them of the peace of God, which through sovereign grace I enjoyed, and these words were that instant in my mind:

> “Christ is my pilot wise, my compass is his word;

My soul each storm defies, while I have such a Lord.

===Chinese===

作者登上一艘开赴加的斯（Cadiz）的海船——险遭海难覆灭——前往马拉加（Malaga）——彼处壮丽恢宏之大教堂——作者与罗马天主教神甫（popish priest）辩论信仰——返英途中自沧海搭救 11 名罹难落魄水手——再度受聘于欧文医生（Doctor Irving）随行前往牙买加（Jamaica）与莫斯基托海岸（Mosquito Shore）——在船上结识一位印第安王子——作者竭力以福音真理训导王子——因船上恶徒之恶劣榜样而受挫——携自牙买加采买之数名奴隶抵达莫斯基托海岸，着手拓荒开辟种植园——莫斯基托印第安人（Mosquito Indians）风俗习尚之记述——作者巧施妙计平息印第安人骚乱——土著设奇宴款待欧文医生与作者，作者作别海岸前往牙买加——遭与其订约搭船者野蛮虐待——逃脱并投奔待其仁厚之莫斯基托海军上将（Mosquito admiral）——转乘他船——饱受恶待之诸般铁证——重逢欧文医生——安抵牙买加——遭船长克扣欺诈——作别医生起程返英。

当我们的海船再次整修齐备即将出海时，船长苦苦哀求我再度随船远航；然而，因我自觉今生所能期许的幸福已莫过于此，起初我断然回绝；但在友人的再三劝导下我最终被说服；怀着对上帝旨意的全然顺服，我于 1775 年 3 月再次登船扬帆开赴加的斯。航程极其顺遂，未历任何重大波折，直至抵近加的斯海湾；某主日正当海船驶向港湾之际，船底猛然撞击暗礁，撞落了一块贴近龙骨的龙骨翼板（garboard plank）。全船顿时乱作一团，水手失声嚎哭，凄厉呼求上帝垂怜赐命。尽管我水性全无且生路断绝，置身此境我内心却毫无畏惧，对尘世生命了无挂碍。我甚至在灵里雀跃欢喜，料定此番身死立将化为永恒荣耀。然而神命定之期尚未届满。身旁的同伴见我这般从容泰然顺服安详，皆大感惊愕；我遂向他们传述自己蒙至高浩瀚恩典所享有的上帝之平安，那一刻圣诗在心中涌现：

> 基督乃我明智领航，圣言即为我之罗盘；
> 既有此等恩主同在，我灵何惧狂涛恶浪。

===Original===

I trust his faithfulness and power,

To save me in the trying hour.

Though rocks and quicksands deep through all my passage lie,

Yet Christ shall safely keep and guide me with his eye.

How can I sink with such a prop,

That bears the world and all things up?”

At this time there were many large Spanish flukers or passage-vessels full of people crossing the channel; who seeing our condition, a number of them came alongside of us. As many hands as could be employed began to work; some at our three pumps, and the rest unloading the ship as fast as possible. There being only a single rock called the Porpus on which we struck, we soon got off it, and providentially it was then high water, we therefore run the ship ashore at the nearest place to keep her from sinking. After many tides, with a great deal of care and industry, we got her repaired again. When we had dispatched our business at Cadiz, we went to Gibraltar, and from thence to Malaga, a very pleasant and rich city, where there is one of the finest cathedrals I had ever seen. It had been above fifty years in building, as I heard, though it was not then quite finished; great part of the inside, however, was completed and highly decorated with the richest marble columns and many superb paintings; it was lighted occasionally by an amazing number of wax tapers of different sizes, some of which were as thick as a man’s thigh; these, however, were only used on some of their grand festivals.
I was very much shocked at the custom of bull-baiting, and other diversions which prevailed here on Sunday evenings, to the great scandal of Christianity and morals. I used to express my abhorrence of it to a priest whom I met with. I had frequent contests about religion with the reverend father, in which he took great pains to make a proselyte of me to his church; and I no less to convert him to mine. On these occasions I used to produce my Bible, and show him in what points his church erred. He then said he had been in England, and that every person there read the Bible, which was very wrong; but I answered him that Christ desired us to search the Scriptures. In his zeal for my conversion, he solicited me to go to one of the universities in Spain, and declared that I should have my education free; and told me, if I got myself made a priest, I might in time become even pope; and that Pope Benedict was a black man.

===Chinese===

> 笃信其信实与大能，
> 拯救我于危难关头。
> 纵使暗礁流沙密布航途，
> 基督目光如炬必护佑引导。
> 有此万物之砥柱托举，
> 我又何致沉沦波涛？

此时恰有许多满载客商的西班牙大型平底渡船（passage-vessels）横渡海峡；见我船大难临头，数艘小艇疾速靠舷驰援。所集人手尽数投入施救：部分在 3 台水泵前舍命抽水，其余人手分秒必争抢运卸除船载货物。幸而所撞暗礁仅为一座名为“江豚礁”（Porpus）的孤立礁石，海船未几便脱离暗礁，且蒙神眷顾恰逢涨潮之机，我们遂将海船冲滩抢搁于最近之平滩，以防沉没深渊。历经数度潮汛，在竭力操劳与细致修葺下，海船终告修复完好。在加的斯办妥公务后，我们开赴直布罗陀（Gibraltar），复转马拉加（Malaga），这是一座令人心旷神怡且极其富庶的名城，耸立着一座我生平所见最为宏伟的大教堂。传闻该堂营建已逾 50 余年，当时仍未全然竣工；然内部主体已告落成，装饰着至为瑰丽的大理石立柱与诸多精美绝伦的油画壁画；堂内以不可胜数的各色巨型蜡烛照耀，部分蜡烛竟粗如男子大腿；然这些蜡烛唯有在盛大宗教节庆之际方始点燃。

对于当地每逢主日傍晚盛行斗牛戏（bull-baiting）及其他世俗娱乐的恶俗，我深感震惊与痛心，这实乃基督教与社会公德之莫大耻辱。我常向结识的一位神甫痛陈内心的反感与唾弃。我与这位尊敬的神甫频频展开宗教辩论，他竭尽全力欲使我皈依罗马天主教；而我亦毫不示弱图谋将其引领归向基督真道。每逢此时我便捧出圣经，向他指出其教会在教义上的偏颇谬误。他便辩称自己曾旅居英格兰，彼处人皆手捧圣经研读，在他看来实属大错特错；我反驳言基督亲自叮嘱我们要查考圣经。见他热切图谋感化我，他甚至力劝我入读西班牙的高等学府，声称愿全免我的学业资费。

===Original===

As I was ever desirous of learning, I paused for some time upon this temptation; and thought by being crafty I might catch some with guile; but I began to think that it would be only hypocrisy in me to embrace his offer, as I could not in conscience conform to the opinions of his church. I was therefore enabled to regard the word of God, which says, “Come out from amongst them,” and refused Father Vincent’s offer. So we parted without conviction on either side.
Having taken at this place some fine wines, fruits, and money, we proceeded to Cadiz, where we took about two tons more of money, etc. and then sailed for England in the month of June. When we were about the north latitude 42, we had contrary wind for several days, and the ship did not make in that time above six or seven miles straight course. This made the captain exceeding fretful and peevish: and I was very sorry to hear God’s most holy name often blasphemed by him. One day, as he was in that impious mood, a young gentleman on board, who was a passenger, reproached him, and said he acted wrong; for we ought to be thankful to God for all things, as we were not in want of anything on board; and though the wind was contrary for us, yet it was fair for some others, who, perhaps, stood in more need of it than we. I immediately seconded this young gentleman with some boldness, and said we had not the least cause to murmur, for that the Lord was better to us than we deserved, and that he had done all things well. I expected that the captain would be very angry with me for speaking, but he replied not a word. However, before that time on the following day, being the 21st of June, much to our great joy and astonishment, we saw the providential hand of our benign Creator, whose ways with his blind creatures are past finding out. The preceding night I dreamed that I saw a boat immediately off the starboard main shrouds; and exactly at half past one o’clock, the following day at noon, while I was below, just as we had dined in the cabin, the man at the helm cried out, A boat! which brought my dream that instant into my mind.

===Chinese===

因我素来渴求学识，面对此等诱惑我沉吟良久；心忖或可将计就计暗施机巧引人归正；然转念一想，自己若接受其提议无异于虚伪欺诈，因在良知上我绝无法苟同其教会之教条。我因之领受了上帝圣言的训诫——“你们务要从他们中间出来”，断然拒绝了文森特神甫（Father Vincent）的提议。于是我们各执己见各奔前程，谁也未曾说服对方。

在此地采办了若干佳酿、水果及现银后，我们折返加的斯，复载约 2 吨现银财物，随后于 6 月份扬帆启程返英。当船行至北纬 42 度附近时，遭遇数日逆风阻遏，整整期间海船直线航程仅推进 6 至 7 英里。这令船长心烦意乱脾气暴躁：听其口中频频亵渎上帝至圣之名，我深感痛心。某日正当他狂悖暴怒之际，船上一名身为旅客的青年绅士当面斥责于他，指其行径荒谬；言我们理当凡事谢恩，因船上物资充裕毫无匮乏；且眼下风向于我们虽是逆风，于他人却正是顺风，而那些人或许远比我们更为迫切需要顺风指引。我随即挺身而出大胆附和这位年轻绅士，言我们毫无理由怨天尤人，因主待我们远胜于我们所配得，且上帝一切的作为尽美尽善。我本料船长必向我大发雷霆，彼却一言不发。然而，就在次日即 6 月 21 日此时之前，令我们大感狂喜与惊异的是，我们亲眼见证了仁慈造物主冥冥之中的奇妙指引，神引领其愚蒙受造物的法则实非凡人所能测透。前一夜我曾做一奇梦，梦见海船右舷主桅张网处紧邻浮现一艘小艇；而次日正午 1 点半整，正当我在底舱与众人在客舱用膳完毕之际，舵手突然失声高呼：“有小艇！”这一声断喝瞬间唤醒了昨夜清晰的梦境。

===Original===

I was the first man that jumped on the deck; and, looking from the shrouds onward, according to my dream, I descried a little boat at some distance; but, as the waves were high, it was as much as we could do sometimes to discern her; we however stopped the ship’s way, and the boat, which was extremely small, came alongside with eleven miserable men, whom we took on board immediately. To all human appearance, these people must have perished in the course of one hour or less, the boat being small, it barely contained them. When we took them up they were half drowned, and had no victuals, compass, water, or any other necessary whatsoever, and had only one bit of an oar to steer with, and that right before the wind; so that they were obliged to trust entirely to the mercy of the waves. As soon as we got them all on board, they bowed themselves on their knees, and, with hands and voices lifted up to heaven, thanked God for their deliverance; and I trust that my prayers were not wanting amongst them at the same time. This mercy of the Lord quite melted me, and I recollected his words, which I saw thus verified in the 107th Psalm “O give thanks unto the Lord, for he is good, for his mercy endureth forever. Hungry and thirsty, their souls fainted in them. They cried unto Lord in their trouble, and he delivered them out of their distresses. And he led them forth by the right way, that they might go to a city of habitation. O that men would praise the Lord for his goodness and for his wonderful works to the children of men! For he satisfieth the longing soul, and filleth the hungry soul with goodness.
“Such as sit in darkness and in the shadow of death:
“Then they cried unto the Lord in their trouble, and he saved them out of their distresses. They that go down to the sea in ships; that do business in great waters: these see the works of the Lord, and his wonders in the deep. Whoso is wise and will observe these things, even they shall understand the loving kindness of the Lord.”
The poor distressed captain said, “that the Lord is good; for, seeing that I am not fit to die, he therefore gave me a space of time to repent.” I was very glad to hear this expression, and took an opportunity when convenient of talking to him on the providence of God.

===Chinese===

我第一个纵身跃上甲板；依梦境所示自桅索极目远眺，赫然望见远方波涛中隐现一艘孤舟；因风浪极高，时而难以辨清其踪影；我们即刻停船下帆减速，那艘狭小无比的小艇随即靠舷，艇上载着 11 名落魄悲惨之水手，我们当即将他们全部接引上船。依常理推断，若再耽搁 1 小时或更短光阴，这群人必葬身鱼腹，因小艇狭窄逼仄已达极限。登船之时众人浑身湿透半陷溺毙，绝粮断水，既无罗盘亦无任何必需物资，唯凭一截残桨掌舵，任凭顺风漂流；唯有全然听凭波涛之摆布。全员登舰之际齐齐屈膝跪倒，手语声嘶仰天高呼，感恩上帝绝处逢生之救赎；我深信那一刻在他们的祈祷中亦交织着我由衷的谢恩。主的浩荡怜悯令我热泪盈眶，我忆及诗篇第 107 篇（107th Psalm）之圣训，在此亲眼见证其应验：“你们要称谢耶和华，因他本为善，他的慈爱永远长存。饥渴交加，他们的心在里面发昏。他们在急难中哀求耶和华，他就从他们的祸患中搭救他们。又领他们行走直路，使他们往可居住的城邑去。但愿人因耶和华的慈爱和他向人所行的奇事都称赞他！因他使心里渴慕的人得以知足，使饥饿的人得饱美物。

那些坐在黑暗中、死荫里的人：

于是他们在急难中哀求耶和华，他就从他们的祸患中拯救他们。在海上坐船，在大水中经理事务的，他们看见耶和华的作为，并在深水中的奇事。凡有智慧的，必在这些事上留心，也必思想耶和华的慈爱。”

那位饱受苦难的遇险船长感慨道：“上主诚然至善；因鉴察我不配身死，特赐我悔改之光阴。”闻听此言我倍感欣慰，借闲暇契机与他深谈神的奇妙护理。

===Original===

They told us they were Portuguese, and were in a brig loaded with corn, which shifted that morning at five o’clock, owing to which the vessel sunk that instant with two of the crew; and how these eleven got into the boat (which was lashed to the deck) not one of them could tell. We provided them with every necessary, and brought them all safe to London: and I hope the Lord gave them repentance unto life eternal.
I was happy once more amongst my friends and brethren, till November, when my old friend, the celebrated Doctor Irving, bought a remarkable fine sloop, about 150 tons. He had a mind for a new adventure in cultivating a plantation at Jamaica and the Mosquito Shore; asked me to go with him, and said that he would trust me with his estate in preference to anyone. By the advice, therefore, of my friends, I accepted of the offer, knowing that the harvest was fully ripe in those parts, and hoped to be the instrument, under God, of bringing some poor sinner to my well beloved master, Jesus Christ. Before I embarked, I found with the Doctor four Mosquito Indians, who were chiefs in their own country, and were brought here by some English traders for some selfish ends. One of them was the Mosquito king’s son; a youth of about eighteen years of age; and whilst he was here he was baptized by the name of George. They were going back at the government’s expense, after having been in England about twelve months, during which they learned to speak pretty good English. When I came to talk to them about eight days before we sailed, I was very much mortified in finding that they had not frequented any churches since they were here, to be baptized, nor was any attention paid to their morals. I was very sorry for this mock Christianity, and had just an opportunity to take some of them once to church before we sailed. We embarked in the month of November 1775, on board of the sloop *Morning Star*, Captain David Miller, and sailed for Jamaica. In our passage, I took all the pains that I could to instruct the Indian prince in the doctrines of Christianity, of which he was entirely ignorant; and, to my great joy, he was quite attentive, and received with gladness the truths that the Lord enabled me to set forth to him. I taught him in the compass of eleven days all the letters, and he could put even two or three of them together and spell them.

===Chinese===

他们告知我们彼等系葡萄牙水手，所乘双桅帆船满载玉米，当日清晨 5 点货物移位倾覆，海船转瞬沉没，2 名船员罹难深渊；而这 11 人究竟何以爬上原先捆扎在甲板上的小艇逃生，无一人能道其原委。我们为他们备齐一切用度，将其全数平安护送抵伦敦：我祈盼上帝已赐予他们通往永生的真切悔改。

我重回英格兰，在知友与弟兄之间尽享团聚之福，直至 11 月份，我那位著名学者旧友欧文医生购入了一艘约 150 吨之精良单桅船。他萌生了一项全新构想，意欲前往牙买加与莫斯基托海岸（Mosquito Shore）拓荒经营种植园；他竭诚邀请我随行，并声称愿将全部产业优先托付于我掌管。在友人的建言下我欣然应允，深知彼方禾场庄稼熟透亟待收割，更祈愿在神手中成为器皿，引渡迷失的罪人归向我挚爱之救主耶稣基督。登船之前，我在医生处结识了 4 名莫斯基托印第安部落酋长，系被数名英国商人出于私利诱拐来英。其中一人系莫斯基托国王之子，年仅 18 岁；旅英期间曾受洗归主，取名乔治（George）。在英格兰盘桓约 12 个月并习得一口流利英语后，彼等正欲借英国政府资助返乡。待我在起航前约 8 天与他们促膝交谈时，令我大感沮丧的是，自领洗之后他们竟从未踏入教堂半步，亦无人过问其品德操守。我对这等有名无实的虚妄信仰深感痛心，在起航前特觅得一次良机带他们去教堂做礼拜。我们于 1775 年 11 月登上戴维·米勒船长（Captain David Miller）统领之“晨星号”（Morning Star）单桅船，扬帆开赴牙买加。航程之中我倾尽全力向这位印第安王子传授基督教教义，彼对此全然蒙昧无知；令我满心欢喜的是，他极具悟性且神情专注，欣然领受上主加添我力所宣讲之真理。短短 11 天之内我便教诲他熟识了所有字母，他甚至能将两三个字母拼合拼读。

===Original===

I had Fox’s *Martyrology* with cuts, and he used to be very fond of looking into it, and would ask many questions about the papal cruelties he saw depicted there, which I explained to him. I made such progress with this youth, especially in religion, that when I used to go to bed at different hours of the night, if he was in his bed, he would get up on purpose to go to prayer with me, without any other clothes than his shirt; and before he would eat any of his meals amongst the gentlemen in the cabin, he would first come to me to pray, as he called it. I was well pleased at this, and took great delight in him, and used much supplication to God for his conversion. I was in full hope of seeing daily every appearance of that change which I could wish; not knowing the devices of satan, who had many of his emissaries to sow his tares as fast as I sowed the good seed, and pull down as fast as I built up. Thus we went on nearly four fifths of our passage, when satan at last got the upper hand. Some of his messengers, seeing this poor heathen much advanced in piety, began to ask him whether I had converted him to Christianity, laughed, and made their jest at him, for which I rebuked them as much as I could; but this treatment caused the prince to halt between two opinions. Some of the true sons of Belial, who did not believe that there was any hereafter, told him never to fear the devil, for there was none existing; and if ever he came to the prince, they desired he might be sent to them. Thus they teased the poor innocent youth, so that he would not learn his book any more! He would not drink nor carouse with these ungodly actors, nor would he be with me, even at prayers. This grieved me very much. I endeavoured to persuade him as well as I could, but he would not come; and entreated him very much to tell me his reasons for acting thus. At last he asked me, “How comes it that all the white men on board who can read and write, and observe the sun, and know all things, yet swear, lie, and get drunk, only excepting yourself?” I answered him, the reason was, that they did not fear God; and that if any one of them died so they could not go to, or be happy with God. He replied, that if these persons went to hell he would go to hell too.

===Chinese===

我随身携有一册带插图之福克斯《殉道者书》（Fox’s Martyrology），他极其喜爱翻阅，屡屡指着书中描绘的罗马教皇残酷迫害信徒之插图连连发问，我皆耐心地为他解惑释疑。我与这位青年的教诲进展神速，尤其在信仰要道之上，以致每逢夜半我分班下榻就寝时，若他已然安睡，只要见我入室便特意起身，只着一件单衣衬衫随我跪地祈祷；每逢在客舱与众绅士同桌进膳之前，他必先奔至我身旁先行同心祈求。对此我大感欣慰，在他身上倾注了无限欢喜，并在神面前频频为他的彻底归信切切代求。我满怀殷切希冀，日日盼望亲见所期许之新生蜕变；孰料撒旦阴险诡诈，广布爪牙趁我播撒良种之际同步抛撒稗子，逢我建造便立加毁拆。航程前五分之四进展顺遂，撒旦最终却占得上风。部分撒旦差役见这可怜的归信异教青年敬虔大进，便开始讥诮讪笑，嘲讽叩问是否已被我点化成了基督徒，我当面严词斥责此等宵小；然这番讥刺却令王子在是非之间徘徊动摇。数名彻头彻尾的悖逆恶徒（sons of Belial）根本不信来世之说，蛊惑他切莫畏惧魔鬼，妄言世间绝无魔鬼存在；声称若恶魔胆敢找上王子，尽管将恶魔引荐予彼等。这群恶徒这般戏弄嘲弄天真无邪的青年，终致使他心灰意冷，再不肯翻开书本研习！他不肯与这群不敬虔之徒饮酒作乐，却亦不再亲近于我，甚至视祈祷为畏途。这令我痛心疾首。我极尽温言规劝，他却避而不见；我再三恳请他吐露这般行事的缘由。他最终反诘于我：“船上那些白人通晓读写、能测日影经纬，洞悉世间万象，却为何无一例外尽皆发咒撒谎、酗酒作恶，独独唯有你一人洁身自好？”我答言缘由尽在彼等心中毫无敬畏上帝之意；若此等人以此恶相身死，断不能面见上帝或享天国之福。他竟答言若这帮白人皆下地狱，他也情愿随之一同坠入地狱。

===Original===

I was sorry to hear this; and, as he sometimes had the toothache, and also some other persons in the ship at the same time, I asked him if their toothache made his easy: he said, No. Then I told him if he and these people went to hell together, their pains would not make his any lighter. This answer had great weight with him: it depressed his spirits much; and he became ever after, during the passage, fond of being alone. When we were in the latitude of Martinico, and near making the land, one morning we had a brisk gale of wind, and, carrying too much sail, the mainmast went over the side. Many people were then all about the deck, and the yards, masts, and rigging, came tumbling all about us, yet there was not one of us in the least hurt, although some were within a hair’s breadth of being killed: and, particularly, I saw two men then, by the providential hand of God, most miraculously preserved from being smashed to pieces. On the fifth of January we made Antigua and Montserrat, and ran along the rest of the islands: and on the fourteenth we arrived at Jamaica. One Sunday while we were there I took the Mosquito Prince George to church, where he saw the sacrament administered. When we came out we saw all kinds of people, almost from the church door for the space of half a mile down to the waterside, buying and selling all kinds of commodities: and these acts afforded me great matter of exhortation to this youth, who was much astonished. Our vessel being ready to sail for the Mosquito shore, I went with the Doctor on board a Guinea-man, to purchase some slaves to carry with us, and cultivate a plantation; and I chose them all my own countrymen. On the twelfth of February we sailed from Jamaica, and on the eighteenth arrived at the Mosquito shore, at a place called Dupeupy. All our Indian guests now, after I had admonished them and a few cases of liquor given them by the Doctor, took an affectionate leave of us, and went ashore, where they were met by the Mosquito king, and we never saw one of them afterwards. We then sailed to the southward of the shore, to a place called Cape Gracias a Dios, where there was a large lagoon or lake, which received the emptying of two or three very fine large rivers, and abounded much in fish and land tortoise.

===Chinese===

听闻此言我不胜哀痛；适逢当时他偶患牙痛，船上亦有数人同遭牙患，我遂问他旁人之牙痛可曾减轻他分毫苦痛：他答言并无减轻。我便乘机告诫他，若他与这帮恶徒同坠地狱，恶徒之剧痛丝毫无法减轻他分毫苦楚。此言对他震动极大；令他心神沮丧萎靡；此后余下航程之中，他皆深居简出离群索居。当船驶抵马提尼克（Martinico）海域接近陆地时，某晨海面刮起狂风，因悬帆过满主桅轰然折断坠海。当时甲板各处满布人丁，横桁、桅杆与缆绳如雨点般倾盆砸落周遭，全船竟无一人遭受重创，尽管数人距丧命仅在一线之间：尤其是其中 2 名水手，在神冥冥护佑之下奇迹般免于被砸成齑粉。1 月 5 日我们眺见安提瓜与蒙特塞拉特（Montserrat），随即沿着岛链航行：并于 14 日平安抵抵牙买加。在牙买加期间的一个主日，我带领莫斯基托王子乔治步入教堂，观摩了神圣圣餐的执守礼仪。步出教堂之际，赫然目睹自教堂大门直至半英里外之水滨，人头攒动摩肩接踵，公开叫卖交易各色货物：此等渎圣行径成了我劝诫这惊异青年的绝佳例证。待海船齐备即将开赴莫斯基托海岸之际，我随欧文医生登上一艘几内亚贩奴船（Guinea-man），采买奴隶随船前往开垦种植园；我所选定之奴隶尽皆系我同族同乡。2 月 12 日我们扬帆驶离牙买加，于 18 日安抵莫斯基托海岸杜佩皮（Dupeupy）。我方印第安宾客在领受我一番叮嘱与医生赏赐之数箱烈酒后，与我们依依惜别登岸，旋即受到莫斯基托国王迎候，此后我们便再未与彼等相遇。我们随后折向海岸以南开赴格拉西亚斯-阿迪奥斯角（Cape Gracias a Dios），彼处有一汪烟波浩渺之大潟湖，承纳两三条壮丽巨川之汇流，盛产各色海鱼与陆龟。

===Original===

Some of the native Indians came on board of us here; and we used them well, and told them we were come to dwell amongst them, which they seemed pleased at. So the Doctor and I, with some others, went with them ashore; and they took us to different places to view the land, in order to choose a place to make a plantation of. We fixed on a spot near a river’s bank, in a rich soil; and, having got our necessaries out of the sloop, we began to clear away the woods, and plant different kinds of vegetables, which had a quick growth. While we were employed in this manner, our vessel went northward to Black River to trade. While she was there, a Spanish guarda costa met with and took her. This proved very hurtful, and a great embarrassment to us. However, we went on with the culture of the land. We used to make fires every night all around us, to keep off wild beasts, which, as soon as it was dark, set up a most hideous roaring. Our habitation being far up in the woods, we frequently saw different kinds of animals; but none of them ever hurt us, except poisonous snakes, the bite of which the Doctor used to cure by giving to the patient, as soon as possible, about half a tumbler of strong rum, with a good deal of Cayenne pepper in it. In this manner he cured two natives and one of his own slaves. The Indians were exceedingly fond of the Doctor, and they had good reason for it; for I believe they never had such an useful man amongst them. They came from all quarters to our dwelling; and some *woolwow*, or flat-headed Indians, who lived fifty or sixty miles above our river, and this side of the South Sea, brought us a good deal of silver in exchange for our goods. The principal articles we could get from our neighbouring Indians, were turtle oil, and shells, little silk grass, and some provisions; but they would not work at anything for us, except fishing; and a few times they assisted to cut some trees down, in order to build us houses; which they did exactly like the Africans, by the joint labour of men, women, and children. I do not recollect any of them to have had more than two wives. These always accompanied their husbands when they came to our dwelling; and then they generally carried whatever they brought to us, and always squatted down behind their husbands. Whenever we gave them anything to eat, the men and their wives ate it separate.

===Chinese===

数名当地印第安土著登船造访；我们极尽礼遇款待，告以意欲在彼等邻里定居辟园，彼等闻言欢欣称善。医生与我遂率数人随其登岸；彼等引领我们游历四方巡视沃土，以期选定开辟种植园之基址。我们最终选定临河平坦沃土处落脚；从单桅船上卸除一应器械家当，随即挥斧伐木开辟林莽，种植数种速生瓜菜。正当我们在岸上操劳之际，海船北上前往黑河（Black River）互市贸易。逗留该地期间猝遭一艘西班牙海岸缉私艇（guarda costa）截获掳走。这予我们以沉重打击，顿陷极大窘境。然而我们依旧矢志开垦沃土。夜夜四下燃起熊熊篝火以御野兽，每逢夜幕低垂密林便传出凄厉骇人之猛兽咆哮。因居所深居密林深处，我们屡屡撞见形形色色之兽类；然除剧毒毒蛇外未曾遭兽害所伤；遇有蛇伤之患，医生惯施妙法施救：第一时间令患者饮下约半玻璃杯烈性朗姆酒，内掺大量开云辣椒粉（Cayenne pepper）。借此神方他先后救治了 2 名土著与 1 名家奴。印第安人对医生崇仰备至，理当如此；因我深信彼等部族世代从未迎来如此大显神手之圣手贤达。四方土著纷至沓来造访寒舍；居于上游 50 至 60 英里、临近南海（即太平洋）之“乌尔瓦”（woolwow）或扁头印第安人，特携大量白银换购我们之货物。我们自邻近土著处所能换购之大宗物产，唯海龟油、龟甲、少许丝草与干粮；除捕鱼外彼等概不愿为我们承担劳役；仅有数次协力伐木建造木屋；其筑造方式与非洲人（Africans）毫无二致，男女老幼齐心协力并肩劳作。据我所知彼等极少有一人娶妻逾 2 人者。造访寒舍之际妻子必紧随夫君身侧；代夫驮运诸般交易之物，安坐时惯常蜷伏于夫君身后。每逢我们赐予食粮，男女夫妻皆分席而食。

===Original===

I never saw the least sign of incontinence amongst them. The women are ornamented with beads, and fond of painting themselves; the men also paint, even to excess, both their faces and shirts: their favourite colour is red. The women generally cultivate the ground, and the men are all fishermen and canoe makers. Upon the whole, I never met any nation that were so simple in their manners as these people, or had so little ornament in their houses. Neither had they, as I ever could learn, one word expressive of an oath. The worst word I ever heard amongst them when they were quarreling, was one that they had got from the English, which was, “you rascal.” I never saw any mode of worship among them; but in this they were not worse than their European brethren or neighbours: for I am sorry to say that there was not one white person in our dwelling, nor anywhere else that I saw in different places I was at on the shore, that was better or more pious than those unenlightened Indians; but they either worked or slept on Sundays: and, to my sorrow, working was too much Sunday’s employment with ourselves; so much so, that in some length of time we really did not know one day from another. This mode of living laid the foundation of my decamping at last. The natives are well made and warlike; and they particularly boast of having never been conquered by the Spaniards. They are great drinkers of strong liquors when they can get them. We used to distil rum from pine apples, which were very plentiful here; and then we could not get them away from our place. Yet they seemed to be singular, in point of honesty, above any other nation I was ever amongst. The country being hot, we lived under an open shed, where we had all kinds of goods, without a door or a lock to any one article; yet we slept in safety, and never lost anything, or were disturbed. This surprised us a good deal; and the Doctor, myself, and others, used to say, if we were to lie in that manner in Europe we should have our throats cut the first night. The Indian governor goes once in a certain time all about the province or district, and has a number of men with him as attendants and assistants. He settles all the differences among the people, like the judge here, and is treated with very great respect.

===Chinese===

在彼等部族之中，我从未见过丝毫放荡不贞之行迹。妇女佩戴各色串珠为饰，极好涂脂抹粉；男子亦酷好彩绘，甚至在其面庞与布衫上涂抹得过分绚烂：最偏爱之色泽乃是赤红。妇人通常在田间耕作，男子则尽数充任渔夫或凿造独木舟。总括而言，我生平从未遇见过民风如此淳朴自然、宅舍如此简陋无饰之民族。据我所知，其语言之中甚至寻不出一个用于发咒诅之词。我所听闻彼等争执之际所吐最恶毒之詈语，不过是自英国人处学来的一句“你这恶棍”（you rascal）。我未曾目睹彼等有任何崇拜神明之仪轨；然在此点上彼等丝毫不比其欧洲（European）弟兄或邻人逊色：因令人扼腕的是，在我们寓所之中乃至我在海岸各处所见之白人，无一人比这些蒙昧之印第安土著更为良善虔诚；每逢主日白人非但不安息，反倒要么劳作要么酣睡：令我痛心的是，周日劳作成了我们自身之常态；以致长久以往，我们甚至彻底丧失了对星期礼拜之辨识。这种生活方式成了促使我最终决意脱离此地之根由。土著体魄强健好勇斗狠；尤以从未遭西班牙人征服而自炫自傲。遇有烈酒必痛饮无度。我们惯用当地极其丰盛之凤梨蒸馏酿造朗姆酒；每逢出酒土著便赖在营地不肯离去。然在忠厚诚信一节上，彼等却远胜我生平所历之任何民族。因气候炎热，我们寄居于敞开式棚舍之中，堆聚各色货物，无一门锁关防；然我们夜夜安枕无忧，未尝折损一针一线，亦未受丝毫惊扰。这令我们大感惊奇；医生、我与众人常叹言，若依此境况露宿欧洲，首夜便恐遭歹人割喉殒命。印第安总督每隔特定时日便巡视其属地部族，随行携大批扈从侍臣。他仿若本地法官调解百姓纷争，备受尊崇景仰。

===Original===

He took care to give us timely notice before he came to our habitation, by sending his stick as a token, for rum, sugar, and gunpowder, which we did not refuse sending; and at the same time we made the utmost preparation to receive his honour and his train. When he came with his tribe, and all our neighbouring chieftains, we expected to find him a grave reverend judge, solid and sagacious; but instead of that, before he and his gang came in sight, we heard them very clamorous; and they even had plundered some of our good neighbouring Indians, having intoxicated themselves with our liquor. When they arrived we did not know what to make of our new guests, and would gladly have dispensed with the honour of their company. However, having no alternative, we feasted them plentifully all the day till the evening; when the governor, getting quite drunk, grew very unruly, and struck one of our most friendly chiefs, who was our nearest neighbour, and also took his gold-laced hat from him. At this a great commotion taken place; and the Doctor interfered to make peace, as we could all understand one another, but to no purpose; and at last they became so outrageous that the Doctor, fearing he might get into trouble, left the house, and made the best of his way to the nearest wood, leaving me to do as well as I could among them. I was so enraged with the Governor, that I could have wished to have seen him tied fast to a tree and flogged for his behaviour; but I had not people enough to cope with his party. I therefore thought of a stratagem to appease the riot. Recollecting a passage I had read in the life of Columbus, when he was amongst the Indians in Mexico or Peru, where, on some occasion, he frightened them, by telling them of certain events in the heavens, I had recourse to the same expedient; and it succeeded beyond my most sanguine expectations. When I had formed my determination, I went in the midst of them; and, taking hold of the Governor, I pointed up to the heavens. I menaced him and the rest: I told them God lived there, and that he was angry with them, and they must not quarrel so; that they were all brothers, and if they did not leave off, and go away quietly, I would take the book (pointing to the Bible), read, and *tell* God to make them dead. This was something like magic.

===Chinese===

他造访寒舍前必提前通报，遣人送来一根权杖木棍为凭，索取朗姆酒、砂糖与火药，我们概不推辞尽数馈赠；与此同时我们倾力备齐诸般礼遇恭候阁下与扈从大驾光临。待他率部族酋长及邻近头领登门之际，我们本期盼瞻仰一位威严端庄、沉稳睿智之法官长者；孰料彼等未入眼帘已然喧声震天；彼等痛饮我们所馈烈酒后酩酊大醉，竟顺道洗劫了邻近友善的印第安邻舍。及至抵埠面对这批新客，我们莫知如何招架，唯愿免此光临之荣幸。然别无退路，只得整日大排筵宴款待至傍晚；待总督烂醉如泥骄横跋扈，悍然掌掴我们最亲善、接壤最近之友邻酋长，并强夺其镶金帽冠。场面登时大乱几欲火并；因彼此皆通晓言辞，医生挺身而出居中调停，奈何徒劳无功；骚乱愈演愈烈几近失控，医生深惧引火烧身，仓皇弃宅奔入最近之树林避难，留下我独自面对狂暴众人力挽狂澜。我对这总督之行径怒不可遏，恨不得将彼绑于树干严加抽挞示惩；然我方势单力薄断难抗衡。危难之际我巧施奇谋平息暴乱。忆及往昔在哥伦布（Columbus）传记中读过之一节，当他在墨西哥或秘鲁与印第安人相处之际，曾借预言天象奇观震慑土著；我遂效仿此法孤注一掷；收效竟远超最狂热之预料。计议已定我昂然步入狂徒正中；一把按住总督，挥臂直指苍穹。我厉声威吓总督与众人：告以上帝在上洞察一切，神正向其震怒，休得在此斗殴争执；彼等皆是手足弟兄，若不立时停手安分离去，我便翻开圣书（手指圣经），诵读圣言并“密告”上帝让彼等立遭暴毙。此言浑如通灵神咒。

===Original===

The clamour immediately ceased, and I gave them some rum and a few other things; after which they went away peaceably; and the Governor afterwards gave our neighbour, who was called Captain Plasmyah, his hat again. When the Doctor returned, he was exceedingly glad at my success in thus getting rid of our troublesome guests. The Mosquito people within our vicinity, out of respect to the Doctor, myself and his people, made entertainments of the grand kind, called in their tongue *tourrie* or *dryckbot*. The English of this expression is, a feast of drinking about, of which it seems a corruption of language. The drink consisted of pine apples roasted, and casades chewed or beaten in mortars; which, after lying some time, ferments, and becomes so strong as to intoxicate, when drank in any quantity. We had timely notice given to us of the entertainment. A white family, within five miles of us, told us how the drink was made, and I and two others went before the time to the village, where the mirth was appointed to be held; and there we saw the whole art of making the drink, and also the kind of animals that were to be eaten there. I cannot say the sight of either the drink or the meat were enticing to me. They had some thousands of pine apples roasting, which they squeezed, dirt and all, into a canoe they had there for the purpose. The casade drink was in beef barrels and other vessels, and looked exactly like hogwash. Men, women, and children, were thus employed in roasting the pine apples, and squeezing them with their hands. For food they had many land torpins or tortoises, some dried turtle, and three large alligators alive, and tied fast to the trees. I asked the people what they were going to do with these alligators; and I was told they were to be eaten. I was much surprised at this, and went home, not a little disgusted at the preparations. When the day of the feast was come, we took some rum with us, and went to the appointed place, where we found a great assemblage of these people, who received us very kindly. The mirth had begun before we came; and they were dancing with music: and the musical instruments were nearly the same as those of any other sable people; but, as I thought, much less melodious than any other nation I ever knew.

===Chinese===

喧嚣骚乱戛然而止，我顺势分发少许朗姆酒与零星杂物；狂徒遂安分和平散去；事后总督亦将镶金帽冠奉还邻舍普拉斯米亚船长（Captain Plasmyah）。待医生归来，见我兵不血刃驱散凶客，欣喜赞叹难以言表。邻近莫斯基托土著为致敬医生、我及同仁，特筹设部族至高盛宴，土语称作“图里”（tourrie）或“德里克博特”（dryckbot）。英语意谓传杯痛饮之聚宴，词句或系辗转讹传而来。宴饮所供之琼浆乃以烤熟之凤梨与在石臼中捣烂或咀嚼之木薯（casades）调和而成；放置片刻自然发酵，烈性逼人稍饮便致酩酊。我们早早接获盛宴之邀。邻近 5 英里处有一户白人人家向我们详述了此酒酿造内情，我与另外两人遂提前赶赴举行盛宴之村落；亲眼目睹了酿酒之全部秘法以及待烹之各色奇珍走兽。坦白而言，无论浆饮或肉食皆难引动我之食欲。彼等架火炙烤数千只凤梨，将连皮带泥之果泥硬生生挤入专设之独木巨舟中。木薯浆则盛于腌牛肉桶及各色大罐中，形质浑若猪食泔水。男女老幼皆投身于炙烤凤梨与徒手挤汁之中。备充佳肴者有大批陆龟海龟，若干干龟肉，以及 3 条牢系于树干之活生生美洲巨鳄。我叩问村民留系巨鳄意欲何为；答言乃是待飨之美馔。我大为错愕归家，对这番备宴筹备心生反胃。及至正日，我们携朗姆酒赶赴指定场地，见当地部族倾巢而出，极尽热忱迎候我们。未至之前欢庆已然启幕；众人伴乐起舞：其乐器与各色黑肤民族大同小异；然在我听来，其悠扬悦耳之韵远逊我平生所闻之任何族群。

===Original===

They had many curious gestures in dancing, and a variety of motions and postures of their bodies, which to me were in no wise attracting. The males danced by themselves, and the females also by themselves, as with us. The Doctor showed his people the example, by immediately joining the women’s party, though not by their choice. On perceiving the women disgusted, he joined the males. At night there were great illuminations, by setting fire to many pine trees, while the dryckbot went round merrily by calabashes or gourds: but the liquor might more justly be called eating than drinking. One Owden, the oldest father in the vicinity, was dressed in a strange and terrifying form. Around his body were skins adorned with different kinds of feathers, and he had on his head a very large and high headpiece, in the form of a grenadier’s cap, with prickles like a porcupine; and he made a certain noise which resembled the cry of an alligator. Our people skipped amongst them out of complaisance, though some could not drink of their tourrie; but our rum met with customers enough, and was soon gone. The alligators were killed and some of them roasted. Their manner of roasting is by digging a hole in the earth, and filling it with wood, which they burn to coal, and then they lay sticks across, on which they set the meat. I had a raw piece of the alligator in my hand: it was very rich: I thought it looked like fresh salmon, and it had a most fragrant smell, but I could not eat any of it. This merrymaking at last ended without the least discord in any person in the company, although it was made up of different nations and complexions. The rainy season came on here about the latter end of May, which continued till August very heavily; so that the rivers were overflowed, and our provisions then in the ground were washed away. I thought this was in some measure a judgment upon us for working on Sundays, and it hurt my mind very much. I often wished to leave this place and sail for Europe; for our mode of procedure and living in this heathenish form was very irksome to me. The word of God saith, “What does it avail a man if he gain the whole world, and lose his own soul?” This was much and heavily impressed on my mind; and, though I did not know how to speak to the Doctor for my discharge, it was disagreeable for me to stay any longer.

===Chinese===

舞蹈身姿多有奇特怪诞之态，躯体扭动起伏千奇百怪，于我看来毫无美感。男女分列各舞，一如我们故土习俗。医生率先垂范，径直步入妇女队伍共舞，然非妇女心甘情愿。见妇女流露不悦，他便转舵步入男子方阵。入夜林间火光冲天，点燃大批松木树桩大放光明，其间“德里克博特”（dryckbot）以葫芦水瓢欢畅流转；然那浓稠浆汁与其谓之豪饮，莫若谓之嚼食更为贴切。邻近最年迈德高之长老名唤奥登（Owden），装扮得怪诞可怖。周身裹缠点缀各色禽羽之兽皮，头顶硕大高耸若掷弹兵帽之冠冕，遍布如豪猪之尖刺利芒；口中发出怪声宛若鳄鱼啼嚎。我方人员出于客套在席间随之蹦跳起舞，纵数人难以下咽其“图里”浆液；然我们随身所携之朗姆酒备受欢迎转瞬罄尽。巨鳄被当场宰杀就地炙烤。其炙烤之道乃掘地为坑，填柴烧成炭火，架设横木置肉其上。我手持一块生鳄鱼肉细审：肉质极其肥美：肌理宛若新鲜三文鱼，且散发幽微清香，然我终究未敢沾唇。这场狂欢盛宴最终在万众和睦融洽中落幕，席间虽汇聚不同民族与肤色，竟无一人挑起是非。约于 5 月底当地进入雨季，倾盆暴雨连绵狂泻直至 8 月；江河泛滥汪洋一片，田间庄稼尽遭洪涛冲刷殆尽。我深感这乃是天主对我们主日违戒劳作之降罚惩戒，内心痛苦万分。我频频渴望脱离这片蛮荒绝境扬帆返欧；因这等异教放浪之生活于我心灵成了沉重桎梏。圣经有云：“人若赚得全世界，赔上自己的生命，有什么益处呢？”此言重重扣击我的心扉；尽管我难以启齿向医生请求开释解聘，再留此地于我已成煎熬。

===Original===

But about the middle of June I took courage enough to ask him for it. He was very unwilling at first to grant my request; but I gave him so many reasons for it, that at last he consented to my going, and gave me the following certificate of my behaviour:

> “The bearer, Gustavus Vassa, has served me several years with strict honesty, sobriety, and fidelity. I can, therefore, with justice recommend him for these qualifications; and indeed in every respect I consider him as an excellent servant. I do hereby certify that he always behaved well, and that he is perfectly trustworthy.
> “Charles Irving.”
> Mosquito Shore, June 15, 1776.

===Chinese===

直至 6 月中旬，我终鼓足勇气向医生提出解职返乡之求。起初他极不情愿恩准所求；然我陈明肺腑诸多因由，他终遂我意，并亲笔为我签署了如下操行褒奖证书：

> “兹证明：持此函者古斯塔夫斯·瓦萨（Gustavus Vassa）侍奉本人多年，操守清廉、严谨克己、忠贞不渝。本人因之理当以其优异品德予以郑重举荐；无论于何种维度，本人皆视其为不可多得之忠仆。特此证明其平素行止端方，全然值得托付信赖。
> 查尔斯·欧文（Charles Irving）
> 莫斯基托海岸，1776 年 6 月 15 日。”

===Original===

Though I was much attached to the doctor, I was happy when he consented. I got everything ready for my departure, and hired some Indians, with a large canoe, to carry me off. All my poor countrymen, the slaves, when they heard of my leaving them, were very sorry, as I had always treated them with care and affection, and did everything I could to comfort the poor creatures, and render their condition easy. Having taken leave of my old friends and companions, on the 18th of June, accompanied by the doctor, I left that spot of the world, and went southward above twenty miles along the river. There I found a sloop, the captain of which told me he was going to Jamaica. Having agreed for my passage with him and one of the owners, who was also on board, named Hughes, the doctor and I parted, not without shedding tears on both sides. The vessel then sailed along the river till night, when she stopped in a lagoon within the same river. During the night a schooner belonging to the same owners came in, and, as she was in want of hands, Hughes, the owner of the sloop, asked me to go in the schooner as a sailor, and said he would give me wages. I thanked him; but I said I wanted to go to Jamaica. He then immediately changed his tone, and swore, and abused me very much, and asked how I came to be freed. I told him, and said that I came into that vicinity with Dr. Irving, whom he had seen that day. This account was of no use; he still swore exceedingly at me, and cursed the master for a fool that sold me my freedom, and the doctor for another in letting me go from him. Then he desired me to go in the schooner, or else I should not go out of the sloop as a freeman. I said this was very hard, and begged to be put on shore again; but he swore that I should not. I said I had been twice amongst the Turks, yet had never seen any such usage with them, and much less could I have expected anything of this kind amongst Christians. This incensed him exceedingly; and, with a volley of oaths and imprecations, he replied, “Christians!
