Damn you, you are one of St. Paul’s men; but by G﻿—﻿—, except you have St. Paul’s or St. Peter’s faith, and walk upon the water to the shore, you shall not go out of the vessel;” which I now found was going amongst the Spaniards towards Carthagena, where he swore he would sell me. I simply asked him what right he had to sell me?

===Chinese===

尽管我对医生怀有深沉依恋，见其慨然允诺我仍满心喜乐。我收拾行装备齐诸般用度，雇来数名手划独木巨舟之印第安人护送出海。我那些沦为奴隶的可怜同胞惊闻我离去皆悲恸欲绝，因我平素待彼等体贴入微仁厚关切，竭尽所能抚慰这群苦难生灵，使其惨境稍得舒缓。告别昔日知友同袍，在医生亲自陪同下，我于 6 月 18 日作别那方天地，沿河向南巡航 20 余英里。彼处停泊着一艘单桅船，船长告我该船正欲开赴牙买加。与船长及同在船上之船东休斯（Hughes）谈妥船资后，医生与我洒泪而别。海船随即沿河顺流航行直至夜幕低垂，在同一河流之潟湖内抛锚停歇。入夜，同属该船东之一艘双桅纵帆船进港，因缺乏水手，单桅船船东休斯劝我登双桅船充任水手，允诺照付工钱。我谢其美意；言自己一心前往牙买加。彼闻言登时变脸，破口大骂污言秽语，厉声质问我究竟何以赎获自由。我据实相告，言自己乃随欧文医生前临此邦，其白日亦曾亲见医生。此番辩白毫无用处；彼愈发变本加厉诅咒辱骂，痛骂卖我自由之前东家乃天下第一号蠢汉，又骂欧文医生放我离去实属糊涂透顶。他勒令我立登双桅船，否则休想以自由人之身跨出单桅船半步。我抗言此乃非理苛待，苦求将其送回岸上；彼立誓咒骂决不应允。我言自己曾两度游历土耳其人之中，从未逢此等野蛮恶行，更料不到基督徒之间竟能干出此等勾当。此言彻底激怒恶徒；连珠炮般掷出滔天诅咒与秽语，咆哮道：“基督徒！去你妈的，你无非是圣保罗门下之徒；指着上帝发誓，除非你身怀圣保罗或圣彼得之通天信心能凌波微步走回岸上，否则休想跨出此船一步！”我此时方惊悉海船正开往卡塔赫纳（Carthagena）西班牙人治下，彼立誓要将我转卖彼处。我质问他凭何名分贩卖于我？

===Original===

but, without another word, he made some of his people tie ropes round each of my ankles, and also to each wrist, and another rope round my body, and hoisted me up without letting my feet touch or rest upon anything. Thus I hung, without any crime committed, and without judge or jury; merely because I was a free man, and could not by the law get any redress from a white person in those parts of the world. I was in great pain from my situation, and cried and begged very hard for some mercy; but all in vain. My tyrant, in a great rage, brought a musket out of the cabin, and loaded it before me and the crew, and swore that he would shoot me if I cried any more. I had now no alternative; I therefore remained silent, seeing not one white man on board who said a word on my behalf. I hung in that manner from between ten and eleven o’clock at night till about one in the morning; when, finding my cruel abuser fast asleep, I begged some of his slaves to slack the rope that was round my body, that my feet might rest on something. This they did at the risk of being cruelly used by their master, who beat some of them severely at first for not tying me when he commanded them. Whilst I remained in this condition, till between five and six o’clock next morning, I trust I prayed to God to forgive this blasphemer, who cared not what he did, but when he got up out of his sleep in the morning was of the very same temper and disposition as when he left me at night. When they got up the anchor, and the vessel was getting under way, I once more cried and begged to be released; and now, being fortunately in the way of their hoisting the sails, they released me. When I was let down, I spoke to one Mr. Cox, a carpenter, whom I knew on board, on the impropriety of this conduct. He also knew the doctor, and the good opinion he ever had of me. This man then went to the captain, and told him not to carry me away in that manner; that I was the doctor’s steward, who regarded me very highly, and would resent this usage when he should come to know it. On which he desired a young man to put me ashore in a small canoe I brought with me.

===Chinese===

彼再无多言，当即命手下水手以粗缆死死绑缚我双踝双腕，复用粗绳牢捆我周身，将我吊悬半空，令我双足悬空不沾片木。我就这般无辜受刑，毫无过犯，未经任何审判；仅仅因我身为自由黑人，而在这方天地里公法绝不容黑人向白人讨取半点公道。悬空之刑令我痛彻心扉，我放声嚎哭苦求宽恕；奈何全属徒劳。施暴狂徒怒气冲天，自客舱抓出一杆火枪，当着我与众水手之面装填弹药，发咒若再敢哀嚎半声立将我当场枪毙。生路断绝我只得紧咬牙关默然饮泣，见全船白人无一人肯为我主持半句公道。我就这般自夜间 10 到 11 点之间吊悬至凌晨 1 点前后；待见恶毒狂徒沉沉睡去，我哀求其麾下数名黑奴稍稍松解捆缚身躯之绳索，使我双足得以稍有着力之处。黑奴冒着遭恶主毒打之奇险伸出援手，此前黑奴因未依命立时捆我，数人曾遭狂徒皮鞭毒打。我在这般绝境中苦熬至翌晨 5 到 6 点之间，内心依旧祈求上帝赦免这不知敬畏之狂悖之徒，待狂徒清晨自梦中醒转，其凶暴戾气与昨夜离去时毫无二致。待全船起锚扬帆备航之际，我再次放声痛哭哀求开释；幸而此时我悬挂之处恰恰阻碍了升帆绳索，彼等方才将我解下。落回甲板，我向船上一位熟识之木匠考克斯先生（Mr. Cox）力陈此等暴行之不义。考克斯亦深知欧文医生素来器重赞赏于我。木匠遂进言船长切莫这般强掳于我；力陈我系医生之总管，医生待我极厚，若得知此番恶行决不善罢甘休。船长闻言遂命一名青年放下我自带之小独木舟，送我登岸。

===Original===

This sound gladdened my heart, and I got hastily into the canoe and set off, whilst my tyrant was down in the cabin; but he soon spied me out, when I was not above thirty or forty yards from the vessel, and, running upon the deck with a loaded musket in his hand, he presented it at me, and swore heavily and dreadfully, that he would shoot me that instant, if I did not come back on board. As I knew the wretch would have done as he said, without hesitation, I put back to the vessel again; but, as the good Lord would have it, just as I was alongside he was abusing the captain for letting me go from the vessel; which the captain returned, and both of them soon got into a very great heat. The young man that was with me now got out of the canoe; the vessel was sailing on fast with a smooth sea: and I then thought it was neck or nothing, so at that instant I set off again, for my life, in the canoe, towards the shore; and fortunately the confusion was so great amongst them on board, that I got out of the reach of the musket shot unnoticed, while the vessel sailed on with a fair wind a different way; so that they could not overtake me without tacking: but even before that could be done I should have been on shore, which I soon reached, with many thanks to God for this unexpected deliverance. I then went and told the other owner, who lived near that shore (with whom I had agreed for my passage) of the usage I had met with. He was very much astonished, and appeared very sorry for it. After treating me with kindness, he gave me some refreshment, and three heads of roasted Indian corn, for a voyage of about eighteen miles south, to look for another vessel. He then directed me to an Indian chief of a district, who was also the Mosquito admiral, and had once been at our dwelling; after which I set off with the canoe across a large lagoon alone (for I could not get anyone to assist me), though I was much jaded, and had pains in my bowels, by means of the rope I had hung by the night before. I was therefore at different times unable to manage the canoe, for the paddling was very laborious. However, a little before dark I got to my destined place, where some of the Indians knew me, and received me kindly. I asked for the admiral; and they conducted me to his dwelling.

===Chinese===

此言如天籁之音令我狂喜，趁凶顽恶徒尚在底舱，我疾步跳入独木舟摇橹疾驰；然方离船 30 或 40 码之遥，恶徒赫然窥见，手执满填弹药之火枪飞奔上甲板，平端枪口死死瞄准于我，连珠炮般发出凄厉诅咒，发誓若不立刻返船立将我轰杀于波涛之中。我深知这恶棍言出必行杀人不眨眼，无奈之下只得转舵重返舷侧；然蒙善主庇佑，正当我靠舷之际，恶徒正怒斥船长擅自放我离船；船长反唇相讥，二人转瞬陷入面红耳赤之激烈争吵。随行青年乘机跳离小舟回船；海船顺风疾驰波平浪静：我心忖死生在此一举，遂借此千钧一发之际拼死划舟冲向岸边；幸喜船上乱作一团，我竟在全无人察觉下安然脱出火枪射程之外，海船在顺风中背道疾驰而去；若欲追赶唯有掉头抢风转舵：而在彼等抢风之前我早已登上海岸，我飞奔登岸，由衷感谢上帝赐下这场猝不及防之绝处逢生。我随即寻访居住水滨之另一位船东（即原先与我订约搭船者），痛陈所历暴行。彼闻言大为惊骇且深表歉疚。他仁厚款待于我，赐我充饥口粮及 3 穗烤熟之印第安玉米，作为南行约 18 英里另寻渡船之口粮。他指点我前往拜谒一位统领属区之印第安酋长，此君亦系莫斯基托海军上将（Mosquito admiral），往昔曾造访我处寒舍；随后我独自一人驾独木小舟横渡浩渺大潟湖（因无一人肯随行相助），因昨夜粗绳吊悬之酷刑，我周身酸痛腹内绞痛难忍。因之数度难操木桨，划水劳役痛苦不堪。然在天黑前夕我终抵目的地，数名相识之印第安人亲切迎候于我。我探问上将行踪；彼等引我步入其帅府。

===Original===

He was glad to see me, and refreshed me with such things as the place afforded; and I had a hammock to sleep in. They acted towards me more like Christians than those whites I was amongst the last night, though they had been baptized. I told the admiral I wanted to go to the next port to get a vessel to carry me to Jamaica; and requested him to send the canoe back which I then had, for which I was to pay him. He agreed with me, and sent five able Indians with a large canoe to carry my things to my intended place, about fifty miles; and we set off the next morning. When we got out of the lagoon and went along shore, the sea was so high that the canoe was oftentimes very near being filled with water. We were obliged to go ashore and drag across different necks of land; we were also two nights in the swamps, which swarmed with mosquito flies, and they proved troublesome to us. This tiresome journey of land and water ended, however, on the third day, to my great joy; and I got on board of a sloop commanded by one Captain Jenning. She was then partly loaded, and he told me he was expecting daily to sail for Jamaica; and having agreed with me to work my passage, I went to work accordingly. I was not many days on board before we sailed; but to my sorrow and disappointment, though used to such tricks, we went to the southward along the Mosquito shore, instead of steering for Jamaica. I was compelled to assist in cutting a great deal of mahogany wood on the shore as we coasted along it, and load the vessel with it, before she sailed. This fretted me much; but, as I did not know how to help myself among these deceivers, I thought patience was the only remedy I had left, and even that was forced. There was much hard work and little victuals on board, except by good luck we happened to catch turtles. On this coast there was also a particular kind of fish called manatee, which is most excellent eating, and the flesh is more like beef than fish; the scales are as large as a shilling, and the skin thicker than I ever saw that of any other fish. Within the brackish waters along shore there were likewise vast numbers of alligators, which made the fish scarce. I was on board this sloop sixteen days, during which, in our coasting, we came to another place, where there was a smaller sloop called the *Indian Queen*, commanded by one John Baker.

===Chinese===

他见我登门欣喜万分，倾其所有备办丰盛饮食款待；并赐我吊床安歇。待我之厚道仁善远逾昨夜那帮领洗之白人。我告知上将欲赴邻近海港搭船前往牙买加；并祈其遣人将我所借独木舟归还失主，资费由我承担。他欣然应诺，立派 5 名精壮印第安勇士驾驭大独木舟，护送我的行装前往约 50 英里外之目击港口；次日清晨我们启程。舟出潟湖沿海岸穿行，恶浪滔天小舟数度濒临灌顶倾覆。我们被迫屡屡靠岸，硬生生拖艇横越数条狭长岬角陆岬；更在蚊虫肆虐之泥淖沼泽中露宿 2 夜，蚊叮虫咬苦不堪言。然历经 3 日水陆兼程之苦途终告安抵，我满心狂喜；登上詹宁船长（Captain Jenning）统领之一艘单桅船。该船已半满装载，船长声称日内即将扬帆开赴牙买加；谈妥以劳役抵充船费后，我当即登船卖力操劳。登船数日海船起锚出海；令我痛心疾首且极度失望的是，尽管深知这帮恶徒惯用骗术，海船竟未驶向牙买加，反而沿着莫斯基托海岸一路向南折返。沿岸巡航期间我被迫卖力砍伐大批桃花心木运送上船，装载完毕方得开航。这令我愤懑难平；然置身这群骗子之中束手无策，自忖唯有忍耐一途，哪怕这忍耐纯系逼迫所致。船上劳役繁重伙食菲薄，除非侥幸捕获海龟充饥。在此海岸更出产一种名为海牛（manatee）之奇鱼，肉质至美，其肉更似牛肉而非鱼鲜；其鳞大如 1 先令硬币，其皮之厚远胜我平生所见任何游鱼。水滨半咸水域鳄鱼密布，致使鱼鲜稀少。我在这艘单桅船上煎熬了 16 天，期间沿岸巡航抵一停泊处，见一艘名为“印第安女王号”（Indian Queen）之小型单桅船，由约翰·贝克（John Baker）统领。

===Original===

He also was an Englishman, and had been a long time along the shore trading for turtle shells and silver, and had got a good quantity of each on board. He wanted some hands very much; and, understanding I was a free man, and wanted to go to Jamaica, he told me if he could get one or two, that he would sail immediately for that island: he also pretended to me some marks of attention and respect, and promised to give me forty-five shillings sterling a month if I would go with him. I thought this much better than cutting wood for nothing. I therefore told the other captain that I wanted to go to Jamaica in the other vessel; but he would not listen to me: and, seeing me resolved to go in a day or two, he got the vessel to sail, intending to carry me away against my will. This treatment mortified me extremely. I immediately, according to an agreement I had made with the captain of the *Indian Queen*, called for her boat, which was lying near us, and it came alongside; and, by the means of a north-pole shipmate which I met with in the sloop I was in, I got my things into the boat, and went on board of the *Indian Queen*, July the 10th. A few days after I was there, we got all things ready and sailed: but again, to my great mortification, this vessel still went to the south, nearly as far as Carthagena, trading along the coast, instead of going to Jamaica, as the captain had promised me: and, what was worst of all, he was a very cruel and bloody-minded man, and was a horrid blasphemer. Among others he had a white pilot, one Stoker, whom he beat often as severely as he did some negroes he had on board. One night in particular, after he had beaten this man most cruelly, he put him into the boat, and made two negroes row him to a desolate key, or small island; and he loaded two pistols, and swore bitterly that he would shoot the negroes if they brought Stoker on board again. There was not the least doubt but that he would do as he said, and the two poor fellows were obliged to obey the cruel mandate; but, when the captain was asleep, the two negroes took a blanket and carried it to the unfortunate Stoker, which I believe was the means of saving his life from the annoyance of insects.

===Chinese===

贝克亦系英格兰人，长年在沿岸巡弋互市收购龟甲与白银，船上已积聚丰厚金银甲贝。他极度渴求人手；得知我系自由民且一心欲赴牙买加，他告以若能再募得一两名水手，立将扬帆开赴牙买加；他更对我虚掷尊崇礼遇之辞，许以每月 45 先令纯银之高俸诚邀我加盟。我自忖此等条件远胜徒劳替人白白伐木。我遂向前任船长请辞欲改乘他船赴牙买加；彼却充耳不闻；见我决意一两日内离去，彼竟起锚扬帆，企图违背我意愿强掳我随船而去。此等行径令我大为光火。依先前与“印第安女王号”船长之契约，我当即高声呼唤停泊在侧之小艇，小艇飞驰靠舷；借着在船上偶遇之一位北极航程旧同袍之掩护协助，我将行李迅速搬入小艇，于 7 月 10 日顺利登上“印第安女王号”。登船数日全船就绪启航：令我极度懊丧的是，该船依旧背道而驰一路向南贸易巡弋，近抵卡塔赫纳（Carthagena），全然背弃开赴牙买加之诺言：尤为恶劣的是，此船长乃是一嗜血残暴、满口亵渎之凶顽狂徒。手下一名名为斯托克（Stoker）之白人引航员，常遭其毒打如虐待黑奴。某夜将斯托克惨无人道毒打一顿后，竟将彼塞入小艇，命 2 名黑奴将其弃置于荒芜孤立之沙洲孤岛；恶主平推双枪，发狠赌咒若敢将斯托克带回立毙二人性命。恶徒言出必行毫无悬念，两位可怜黑奴唯命是从；然待船长酣睡之后，两位黑奴暗携一领毛毯送予不幸之斯托克，此举深信拯救了斯托克免遭毒虫噬咬殒命。

===Original===

A great deal of entreaty was used with the captain the next day, before he would consent to let Stoker come on board; and when the poor man was brought on board he was very ill, from his situation during the night, and he remained so till he was drowned a little time after. As we sailed southward we came to many uninhabited islands, which were overgrown with fine large coconuts. As I was very much in want of provisions, I brought a boat load of them on board, which lasted me and others for several weeks, and afforded us many a delicious repast in our scarcity. One day, before this, I could not help observing the providential hand of God, that ever supplies all our wants, though in the ways and manner we know not. I had been a whole day without food, and made signals for boats to come off, but in vain. I therefore earnestly prayed to God for relief in my need; and at the close of the evening I went off the deck. Just as I laid down I heard a noise on the deck; and, not knowing what it meant, I went directly on the deck again, when what should I see but a fine large fish about seven or eight pounds, which had jumped aboard! I took it, and admired, with thanks, the good hand of God; and, what I considered as not less extraordinary, the captain, who was very avaricious, did not attempt to take it from me, there being only him and I on board; for the rest were all gone ashore trading. Sometimes the people did not come off for some days: this used to fret the captain, and then he would vent his fury on me by beating me, or making me feel in other cruel ways. One day especially, in his wild, wicked, and mad career, after striking me several times with different things, and once across my mouth, even with a red burning stick out of the fire, he got a barrel of gunpowder on the deck, and swore that he would blow up the vessel. I was then at my wit’s end, and earnestly prayed to God to direct me. The head was out of the barrel; and the captain took a lighted stick out of the fire to blow himself and me up, because there was a vessel then in sight coming in, which he supposed was a Spaniard, and he was afraid of falling into their hands. Seeing this I got an axe, unnoticed by him, and placed myself between him and the powder, having resolved in myself as soon as he attempted to put the fire in the barrel to chop him down that instant.

===Chinese===

次日众人百般苦苦求情，船长方勉强恩准接引斯托克归舰；这可怜人被抬上甲板时因彻夜受冻受辱已奄奄一息，此后一病不起直至不久后溺水殒命。海船向南航行途经诸多荒无人烟之孤岛，岛上盛产硕大甘美之椰子。因极度匮乏口粮，我采运了满艇椰子载上甲板，敷衍我和同袍数周之需，在饥荒绝境中成了无上甘美之盛馔。在此之前某日，我由衷见证了上帝奇妙之眷顾，神总在我们未知之幽微处供应所需。我曾整整一日水米未进，频频打信号招引小舟靠舷皆无功而返。我遂在急难中向神切切求告；傍晚收工下甲板就寝。方一躺下忽闻甲板异响；莫知何故复登甲板查看，赫然见一条约 7 至 8 磅之肥美鲜鱼自跃登舟！我捧鱼赞叹由衷谢恩神之恩赐；更显奇妙的是，贪婪成性之船长竟未向我强索该鱼，当时船上唯有他与我二人留守；其余人丁尽皆登岸贸易。有时贸易船员数日未归：船长因之焦躁暴怒，便将滔天怒火全数倾泻于我身，非打即骂或施以诸般残酷折磨。尤有某日，正当其陷入癫狂暴怒之狂悖之际，操起诸般凶器劈头盖脸痛殴于我，甚至自炉火抓出一根烧得通红之火棍猛抽我嘴唇，复从底舱搬出一整桶火药置于甲板，立誓要将整艘海船炸上青天。我吓得魂飞魄散，在心中切切祷告祈求上帝指点迷津。火药桶已揭开盖木；船长手持熊熊燃烧之火炭欲同归于尽，因当时正有一艘疑似西班牙海船破浪而来，彼深惧沦为战俘。见此凶兆我悄然摸来一把利斧，侧身横亘于彼与火药桶之间，暗暗咬牙立誓一旦其火炭触碰木桶，立时挥斧将其当场劈杀。

===Original===

I was more than an hour in this situation; during which he struck me often, still keeping the fire in his hand for this wicked purpose. I really should have thought myself justifiable in any other part of the world if I had killed him, and prayed to God, who gave me a mind which rested solely on himself. I prayed for resignation, that his will might be done; and the following two portions of his holy word, which occurred to my mind, buoyed up my hope, and kept me from taking the life of this wicked man. “He hath determined the times before appointed, and set bounds to our habitations,” Acts 17:26. And, “Who is there amongst you that feareth the Lord, that obeyeth the voice of his servant, that walketh in darkness and hath no light? let him trust in the name of the Lord, and stay upon his God,” Isaiah 1:20. And thus by the grace of God I was enabled to do. I found him a present help in the time of need, and the captain’s fury began to subside as the night approached: but I found,

> “That he who cannot stem his anger’s tide

Doth a wild horse without a bridle ride.”

===Chinese===

我就这般在生死悬于一线之境僵持逾 1 个钟头；期间他屡屡击打于我，手握烈火炭块誓成恶谋。若在世间任何他处，我自卫将其斩杀皆属理所应当，我向神切切祷告，上帝赐我安卧于其怀抱之泰然心境。我祈求顺服领受神之旨意；那一刻圣经两处经文如甘霖浮现心头，支撑起我的希望，阻遏了我斩杀这恶徒之手——“他从一本造出万族的人，住在全地上，并且预先定准他们的年限和所住的疆界”，见《使徒行传》17:26（Acts 17:26）；以及“你们中间谁是敬畏耶和华、听从他仆人的话？这人行在暗中，没有亮光，当倚靠耶和华的名，仗赖自己的神”，见《以赛亚书》1:20（Isaiah 1:20）。借着上帝的浩瀚恩典我得以恪守此训。在患难关头我体认神乃随时的帮助，暮色低垂船长之癫狂戾气渐趋消退：然而我深悟：

> “怒海滔天若难遏，
> 宛若无辔骑狂驹。”

===Original===

The next morning we discovered that the vessel which had caused such a fury in the captain was an English sloop. They soon came to an anchor where we were, and, to my no small surprise, I learned that Doctor Irving was on board of her on his way from the Mosquito shore to Jamaica. I was for going immediately to see this old master and friend, but the captain would not suffer me to leave the vessel. I then informed the doctor, by letter, how I was treated, and begged that he would take me out of the sloop: but he informed me that it was not in his power, as he was a passenger himself; but he sent me some rum and sugar for my own use. I now learned that after I had left the estate which I managed for this gentleman on the Mosquito shore, during which the slaves were well fed and comfortable, a white overseer had supplied my place: this man, through inhumanity and ill-judged avarice, beat and cut the poor slaves most unmercifully; and the consequence was, that everyone got into a large Puriogua canoe, and endeavoured to escape; but not knowing where to go, or how to manage the canoe, they were all drowned; in consequence of which the doctor’s plantation was left uncultivated, and he was now returning to Jamaica to purchase more slaves and stock it again. On the 14th of October the *Indian Queen* arrived at Kingston in Jamaica. When we were unloaded I demanded my wages, which amounted to eight pounds and five shillings sterling; but Captain Baker refused to give me one farthing, although it was the hardest-earned money I ever worked for in my life. I found out Doctor Irving upon this, and acquainted him of the captain’s knavery. He did all he could to help me to get my money; and we went to every magistrate in Kingston (and there were nine), but they all refused to do anything for me, and said my oath could not be admitted against a white man. Nor was this all; for Baker threatened that he would beat me severely if he could catch me for attempting to demand my money; and this he would have done, but that I got, by means of Dr. Irving, under the protection of Captain Douglas of the *Squirrel* man of war. I thought this exceedingly hard usage; though indeed I found it to be too much the practice there to pay free men for their labour in this manner. One day I went with a free negroe tailor, named Joe Diamond, to one Mr.

===Chinese===

次日清晨我们赫然发觉，那艘令船长吓破胆之海船竟是一艘英国单桅船。该船未几便在我们近侧抛锚停歇，令我惊喜交加的是，我获悉欧文医生正在船上，正取道莫斯基托海岸开赴牙买加。我意欲登船拜望这位旧东家与良友，凶蛮船长却严禁我离船半步。我遂手书一封，详述饱受虐待之惨状，乞请医生助我脱困离开此船：医生回信言彼身为乘客亦无能为力；然特意送来若干朗姆酒与砂糖供我支用。我此时方知，自我在莫斯基托海岸离弃所代管之种植园后（在任期间家奴温饱安适），一名白人监工接替了我的职位；此人残暴贪婪毫无心肝，对可怜黑奴施以酷刑毒打；终致全体奴隶盗取一艘名为“皮罗格”（Puriogua）之大型独木舟企图集体逃亡；然因辨不清方向且不善操舟，全员尽皆淹毙汪洋；医生的种植园因之荒芜废弃，他此行正欲重返牙买加采买新奴重建庄园。10 月 14 日“印第安女王号”安抵牙买加金斯敦（Kingston）。卸货告竣我索讨工钱，本应支取 8 镑 5 先令纯银；贝克船长却分文不给，尽管此乃我平生所赚最为艰辛之血汗钱。我遂寻访欧文医生，痛陈船长之奸诈无赖。医生竭尽全力助我讨薪；我们奔走于金斯敦全城每一位治安官席前（共 9 位治安法官），然全员皆拒绝受理，声称黑人之誓词绝不可作为指控白人之法庭供证。不仅如此；贝克甚至扬言一旦撞见我索讨工钱必将我打得皮开肉绽；而若非借欧文医生之庇护引荐，投奔“松鼠号”（Squirrel）战舰道格拉斯舰长（Captain Douglas）麾下，彼早已对我施暴。我深感世态炎凉手段恶毒；然在彼方天地，以这等无赖手段克扣自由黑人之血汗酬劳，早已蔚然成风。某日我随一名名为乔·戴蒙德（Joe Diamond）之自由黑人裁缝前往考克伦先生（Mr. Cochran）门下讨要区区微薄欠薪。

===Original===

Cochran, who was indebted to him some trifling sum; and the man, not being able to get his money, began to murmur. The other immediately took a horsewhip to pay him with it; but, by the help of a good pair of heels, the tailor got off. Such oppressions as these made me seek for a vessel to get off the island as fast as I could; and by the mercy of God I found a ship in November bound for England, when I embarked with a convoy, after having taken a last farewell of Doctor Irving. When I left Jamaica he was employed in refining sugars; and some months after my arrival in England I learned, with much sorrow, that this my amiable friend was dead, owing to his having eaten some poisoned fish. We had many very heavy gales of wind in our passage; in the course of which no material incident occurred, except that an American privateer, falling in with the fleet, was captured and set fire to by his Majesty’s ship the *Squirrel*. On January the seventh, 1777, we arrived at Plymouth. I was happy once more to tread upon English ground; and, after passing some little time at Plymouth and Exeter among some pious friends, whom I was happy to see, I went to London with a heart replete with thanks to God for all past mercies.

===Chinese===

考克伦先生因索讨欠薪恼羞成怒，竟拔出马鞭痛抽裁缝以代薪金；裁缝全凭步履如飞方侥幸脱逃。此等骇人听闻之压迫凌辱，促使我发疯般寻觅出海船只以期火速逃离该岛；蒙神怜悯，我终在 11 月份寻得一艘归国商船，随护航舰队扬帆出海，此前我与欧文医生作了人生最后的诀别。辞别牙买加时他正投身于精炼食糖之产业；返英数月后我惊闻痛心噩耗，这位温厚仁善的知友已不幸辞世，因误食某种剧毒毒鱼中毒暴毙。归国航程饱经狂风恶浪；途中未历重大事故，唯有一艘美国私掠武装商船（American privateer）闯入我护航舰队，被英皇陛下之“松鼠号”战舰当场俘获并纵火焚毁。1777 年 1 月 7 日我们安抵普利茅斯（Plymouth）。重履英格兰故土令我满心欢畅；在普利茅斯与埃克塞特（Exeter）拜会数位虔诚敬主之故友并小住数日后，我怀着对上帝往昔浩瀚恩典之无尽谢忱，欣然返回伦敦。

## XII / 第十二章
===Original===

Different transactions of the author’s life till the present time﻿—His application to the late Bishop of London to be appointed a missionary to Africa﻿—Some account of his share in the conduct of the late expedition to Sierra Leona﻿—Petition to the Queen﻿—Conclusion.
Such were the various scenes which I was a witness to, and the fortune I experienced until the year 1777. Since that period my life has been more uniform, and the incidents of it fewer, than in any other equal number of years preceding; I therefore hasten to the conclusion of a narrative, which I fear the reader may think already sufficiently tedious.
I had suffered so many impositions in my commercial transactions in different parts of the world, that I became heartily disgusted with the seafaring life, and I was determined not to return to it, at least for some time. I therefore once more engaged in service shortly after my return, and continued for the most part in this situation until 1784.
Soon after my arrival in London, I saw a remarkable circumstance relative to African complexion, which I thought so extraordinary, that I beg leave just to mention it: A white negro woman, that I had formerly seen in London and other parts, had married a white man, by whom she had three boys, and they were every one mulattoes, and yet they had fine light hair. In 1779 I served Governor Macnamara, who had been a considerable time on the coast of Africa. In the time of my service, I used to ask frequently other servants to join me in family prayers; but this only excited their mockery. However, the Governor, understanding that I was of a religious turn, wished to know of what religion I was; I told him I was a protestant of the church of England, agreeable to the thirty-nine articles of that church, and that whomsoever I found to preach according to that doctrine, those I would hear. A few days after this, we had some more discourse on the same subject: the Governor spoke to me on it again, and said that he would, if I chose, as he thought I might be of service in converting my countrymen to the Gospel faith, get me sent out as a missionary to Africa.

===Chinese===

作者生平诸般遭际直至当下之记述——作者呈请前任伦敦主教（Bishop of London）委任其为赴非洲传教士（missionary to Africa）——作者参与近期塞拉利昂（Sierra Leone）远征探险之始末——呈递王后请愿书——全书结语。

凡此诸般，皆系我截至 1777 年所亲眼目睹之沧桑变迁与亲身经历之命运沉浮。自该时期以降，我的生平际遇渐趋平稳安详，波折变故亦较以往任何同等年岁大为减少；因之我急切欲为此篇自传画上句点，唯恐读者诸君早已深感冗长乏味。

因昔年在世界各地经商营运之中屡遭欺凌讹诈，我对涉海生涯深恶痛绝，决意再不重操舟楫，至少在一段时日内断不再涉沧波。于是在返英后不久我再度受雇充当侍从仆役，在此职位上安稳操劳直至 1784 年。

抵伦敦后不久，我目睹了一桩关涉非洲人（African）肤色之奇特变故，觉其殊异非凡，容我不揣冒昧略作记述：有一名我往昔在伦敦及其他地方见过的白人黑人妇女（患白化病之黑人），嫁给了一名白人男子，婚后诞下 3 名男童，3 人尽皆系混血儿（mulattoes），却生有一头漂亮浅色金发。1779 年我侍奉麦克纳马拉总督（Governor Macnamara），他曾在非洲海岸驻节多年。侍奉期间，我频频邀请其他仆役随我一同参与家庭晚祷；此举反招致彼等之肆意嘲弄。然总督察知我笃信宗教，欲深究我究竟奉何宗教；我告以自己系英国国教（church of England）之新教徒，恪守该教会之三十九条信纲（thirty-nine articles），凡依此教义宣讲真道者，我皆乐于受教。数日后我们重叙此题：总督再次与我深谈，言及若我心甘情愿，因念及我或可为引领同胞归信福音真道尽一臂之力，他愿设法保荐我作为传教士差遣前往非洲。

===Original===

I at first refused going, and told him how I had been served on a like occasion by some white people the last voyage I went to Jamaica, when I attempted (if it were the will of God) to be the means of converting the Indian prince; and I said I supposed they would serve me worse than Alexander the coppersmith did St. Paul, if I should attempt to go amongst them in Africa. He told me not to fear, for he would apply to the Bishop of London to get me ordained. On these terms I consented to the Governor’s proposal to go to Africa, in hope of doing good if possible amongst my countrymen; so, in order to have me sent out properly, we immediately wrote the following letters to the late Bishop of London:

> Showeth,
> That your memorialist is a native of Africa, and has a knowledge of the manners and customs of the inhabitants of that country.
> That your memorialist has resided in different parts of Europe for twenty-two years last past, and embraced the Christian faith in the year 1759.
> That your memorialist is desirous of returning to Africa as a missionary, if encouraged by your Lordship, in hopes of being able to prevail upon his countrymen to become Christians; and your memorialist is the more induced to undertake the same, from the success that has attended the like undertakings when encouraged by the Portuguese through their different settlements on the coast of Africa, and also by the Dutch: both governments encouraging the blacks, who, by their education are qualified to undertake the same, and are found more proper than European clergymen, unacquainted with the language and customs of the country.
> Your memorialist’s only motive for soliciting the office of a missionary is, that he may be a means, under God, of reforming his countrymen and persuading them to embrace the Christian religion. Therefore your memorialist humbly prays your Lordship’s encouragement and support in the undertaking.
> Gustavus Vassa.
> At Mr. Guthrie’s, tailor,

No. 17, Hedge-lane.

===Chinese===

起初我断然拒绝前往，向他陈明上次航行牙买加期间，正当我图谋（若蒙神旨意）引领印第安王子归信基督之际，某些白人是如何以此为借口残酷戏弄于我；我言若自己踏上非洲土地涉足彼等之间，彼等待我之狠辣恐将远甚当年铜匠亚历山大（Alexander the coppersmith）戕害圣保罗。总督力劝我切莫畏惧，声称愿代我奏请伦敦主教为我行按立圣职之礼。在此承诺之下，我欣然应允总督之保荐开赴非洲，期盼在同胞同族之中竭尽微薄之力广行善德；为使我的差遣名正言顺，我们当即联名向已故伦敦主教呈递了如下呈文：

> 谨呈文列明：
> 呈文人系非洲土著，深谙该国之风俗习尚与民情土壤。
> 呈文人在过去 22 年间旅居欧洲各地，并于 1759 年领洗归信基督教真道。
> 呈文人深切渴盼在主教阁下之鼓励赞助下，以传教士之身重返非洲，期冀劝导同胞归依基督真道；且促使呈文人承当此项大业者，尤在葡萄牙人于非洲海岸各拓殖点推行此等善举所获之奇功，以及荷兰人推行此法之盛誉：此两葡荷政府皆大力延揽黑人子民，彼等受过良好教育足以胜任此职，且相较于不谙当地语言习俗之欧洲教士（European clergymen），黑人教职人员实显尤为得体适宜。
> 呈文人恳请获授传教圣职之唯一动机，乃愿在上帝手中化为器皿，移风易俗归正同胞，劝导彼等皈依基督圣教。因之呈文人卑微恳请主教阁下对此义举赐予鼓励与护佑提携。
> 古斯塔夫斯·瓦萨（Gustavus Vassa）
> 寄寓裁缝加思里先生（Mr. Guthrie）处，
> 树篱巷 17 号（No. 17, Hedge-lane）

===Original===

> My Lord,
> I have resided near seven years on the coast of Africa, for most part of the time as commanding officer. From the knowledge I have of the country and its inhabitants, I am inclined to think that the within plan will be attended with great success, if countenanced by your Lordship. I beg leave further to represent to your Lordship, that the like attempts, when encouraged by other governments, have met with uncommon success; and at this very time I know a very respectable character a black priest at Cape Coast Castle. I know the within named Gustavus Vassa, and believe him a moral good man.
> I have the honour to be, my Lord,

Your Lordship’s

Humble and obedient servant,

**Matt Macnamara**.
> Grove, 11th March 1779.

This letter was also accompanied by the following from Doctor Wallace, who had resided in Africa for many years, and whose sentiments on the subject of an African mission were the same with Governor Macnamara’s.

> My Lord,
> I have resided near five years on Senegambia on the coast of Africa, and have had the honour of filling very considerable employments in that province. I do approve of the within plan, and think the undertaking very laudable and proper, and that it deserves your Lordship’s protection and encouragement, in which case it must be attended with the intended success.
> I am, my Lord, your Lordship’s

Humble and obedient servant,

**Thomas Wallace**.

===Chinese===

> 主教阁下：
> 本人曾旅居非洲海岸近 7 年之久，期间多半出任统兵长官。凭本人对该国风土民情之深切体察，深信附呈之宏图大略若蒙阁下恩准俯纳，定能大获全胜。本人谨向阁下申明，类似之尝试在他国政府护佑推行下，皆曾大获非凡之奇功；且值此时刻，本人亲睹在开普海岸城堡（Cape Coast Castle）便有一位享有极高令名尊荣之黑人司铎。本人熟识附呈所列之古斯塔夫斯·瓦萨，深知其品德端方乃一厚道贤达之良善君子。
> 顺致崇高敬意，主教阁下，
> 阁下卑微顺服之仆，
> 马特·麦克纳马拉（Matt Macnamara）
> 格罗夫，1779 年 3 月 11 日。

随同此信函一同附呈者，尚有华莱士医生（Doctor Wallace）之推荐函，华莱士医生在非洲旅居多年，在非洲宣教大业上其见解与麦克纳马拉总督不谋而合。

> 主教阁下：
> 本人曾旅居非洲海岸之塞内冈比亚（Senegambia）近 5 年之久，并有幸在该省履职要津。本人深切赞许附呈之方略，深信此项义举崇高得体，理当荣蒙阁下之荫庇与提携，若获阁下首肯，必能收获预期之圆满大获成功。
> 谨此，主教阁下，
> 阁下卑微顺服之仆，
> 托马斯·华莱士（Thomas Wallace）

===Original===

With these letters, I waited on the Bishop by the Governor’s desire, and presented them to his Lordship. He received me with much condescension and politeness; but, from some certain scruples of delicacy, declined to ordain me.
My sole motive for thus dwelling on this transaction, or inserting these papers, is the opinion which gentlemen of sense and education, who are acquainted with Africa, entertain of the probability of converting the inhabitants of it to the faith of Jesus Christ, if the attempt were countenanced by the legislature.
Shortly after this I left the Governor, and served a nobleman in the Devonshire militia, with whom I was encamped at Coxheath for some time; but the operations there were too minute and uninteresting to make a detail of.
In the year 1783 I visited eight counties in Wales, from motives of curiosity. While I was in that part of the country I was led to go down into a coal-pit in Shropshire, but my curiosity nearly cost me my life; for while I was in the pit the coals fell in, and buried one poor man, who was not far from me: upon this I got out as fast as I could, thinking the surface of the earth the safest part of it.
In the spring 1784 I thought of visiting old ocean again. In consequence of this I embarked as steward on board a fine new ship called the *London*, commanded by Martin Hopkin, and sailed for New-York. I admired this city very much; it is large and well-built, and abounds with provisions of all kinds. While we lay here a circumstance happened which I thought extremely singular:﻿—One day a malefactor was to be executed on a gallows; but with a condition that if any woman, having nothing on but her shift, married the man under the gallows, his life was to be saved. This extraordinary privilege was claimed; a woman presented herself; and the marriage ceremony was performed. Our ship having got laden we returned to London in January 1785. When she was ready again for another voyage, the captain being an agreeable man, I sailed with him from hence in the spring, March 1785, for Philadelphia. On the fifth of April we took our departure from the Land’s-end, with a pleasant gale; and about nine o’clock that night the moon shone bright, and the sea was smooth, while our ship was going free by the wind, at the rate of about four or five miles an hour.

===Chinese===

怀揣此两封荐信，我遵总督之嘱拜谒伦敦主教，并将信函呈递主教阁下案前。主教接见我时极尽客气谦和温厚；然出于某种顾虑与审慎考虑，最终婉拒为我施按立礼。

我之所以这般不厌其详叙述此番交涉、录入此等公文，唯一动机在于向世人展示：凡通晓非洲实情、明达事理且受过良好教养之绅士名流，皆深信一旦得国家立法机关支持，引领非洲万民归信耶稣基督信仰乃是大有可为之举。

此后不久我辞别总督，转入德文郡民兵团（Devonshire militia）一位贵族门下效力，随其在考克斯希思（Coxheath）扎营驻扎了一段时日；然彼处之操演细枝末节平淡无奇，不足挂齿。

1783 年，出于探奇寻胜之兴致，我游历了威尔士 8 个郡。在此期间，因好奇心驱使我曾步入什罗普郡（Shropshire）之一座煤矿井下探幽，然而这番好奇心几令我丧命黄泉；因正当我置身矿井深处之际，煤层轰然坍塌，当场将距我不远之一名可怜矿工活埋殉难：见此惨状我连滚带爬逃出矿坑，深感大地的地表才是世间最稳妥之居所。

1784 年春，我再次萌生探访老沧海之念。我于是在一艘名为“伦敦号”（London）之崭新精良大船上充任管事侍从，该船由马丁·霍普金（Martin Hopkin）统领，扬帆开赴纽约（New-York）。我对这座名城赞叹不已；城市广袤繁华建筑巍峨，且充盈各色丰盛粮秣物资。我们驻泊该地期间曾发生一桩极其离奇之轶事：某日一名死囚即将押赴绞刑架行刑；然按例若有任何妇人仅着一件贴身单衣衬衫，愿在绞刑架下当场委身下嫁该男子，便可免除其死刑保全性命。此等殊异之特权果真被当场认领；一名女子昂然步入刑场；绞刑架下之成婚大礼当场告竣。海船装载告竣，我们于 1785 年 1 月安返伦敦。待海船再度整备齐备出海之际，因船长待人极其和蔼可亲，我于 1785 年 3 月春季再次随其扬帆出海，开赴费城（Philadelphia）。4 月 5 日我们在怡人顺风中驶离兰兹角（Land’s-end）；当晚 9 点前后月华皎洁，海面风平浪静，海船乘风破浪疾驰，航速约为每小时 4 至 5 英里。

===Original===

At this time another ship was going nearly as fast as we on the opposite point, meeting us right in the teeth, yet none on board observed either ship until we struck each other forcibly head and head, to the astonishment and consternation of both crews. She did us much damage, but I believe we did her more; for when we passed by each other, which we did very quickly, they called to us to bring to, and hoist out our boat, but we had enough to do to mind ourselves; and in about eight minutes we saw no more of her. We refitted as well as we could the next day, and proceeded on our voyage, and in May arrived at Philadelphia. I was very glad to see this favourite old town once more; and my pleasure was much increased in seeing the worthy quakers freeing and easing the burdens of many of my oppressed African brethren. It rejoiced my heart when one of these friendly people took me to see a free-school they had erected for every denomination of black people, whose minds are cultivated here and forwarded to virtue; and thus they are made useful members of the community. Does not the success of this practice say loudly to the planters in the language of scripture﻿—“Go ye and do likewise?”
In October 1785 I was accompanied by some of the Africans, and presented this address of thanks to the gentlemen called Friends or Quakers, in Gracechurch-Court Lombard-Street:

===Chinese===

此时正有一艘巨舰以相近之疾速自迎面逆向对开而来，迎头直撞，然两船值守水手皆未察觉对方，直至两舰迎头猛烈相撞，轰然巨响令两船船员惊骇欲绝。对方将我船撞成重创，然我深信我船予彼之重创更甚；两船交错而过之际，电光石火间对方高呼命我们停船并放下救生艇，奈何我们自身难保自顾不暇；约 8 分钟后便再不见对方踪影。次日我们倾力修葺创口，继续破浪前行，于 5 月平安抵达费城。重游这方令人心醉之旧地名城我满心欢畅；目睹可敬之贵格会信徒（quakers）全力为诸多受压迫之非洲弟兄赎身解缚减免重负，更令我喜不自胜。当一位仁厚信友携我参观其专为各阶层黑人学童筹建之免费义学时，我的心潮激荡不已；黑人孩童在此受到智识开导涵养美德，从而化为造福社会之有用栋梁。此等善政之奇功，难道不是以圣经圣言向种植园主发出震耳欲聋之当头棒喝——“你们去，也照样行吧”？

1785 年 10 月，在数名非洲同胞陪同下，我前往伦巴第街恩典教堂苑（Gracechurch-Court Lombard-Street），向被称为公谊会或贵格会（Friends or Quakers）之众位仁人君子呈递了如下感恩致敬信：

===Original===

> Gentlemen,
> By reading your book, entitled a *Caution to Great Britain and Her Colonies, Concerning the Calamitous State of the Enslaved Negroes*: We the poor, oppressed, needy, and much-degraded negroes, desire to approach you with this address of thanks, with our inmost love and warmest acknowledgment; and with the deepest sense of your benevolence, unwearied labour, and kind interposition, towards breaking the yoke of slavery, and to administer a little comfort and ease to thousands and tens of thousands of very grievously afflicted, and too heavy burdened negroes.
> Gentlemen, could you, by perseverance, at last be enabled, under God, to lighten in any degree the heavy burden of the afflicted, no doubt it would, in some measure, be the possible means, under God, of saving the souls of many of the oppressors; and, if so, sure we are that the God, whose eyes are ever upon all his creatures, and always rewards every true act of virtue, and regards the prayers of the oppressed, will give to you and yours those blessings which it is not in our power to express or conceive, but which we, as a part of those captived, oppressed, and afflicted people, most earnestly wish and pray for.

===Chinese===

> 诸位仁人君子：
> 借由研读贵会所著题为《告大不列颠及其殖民地书：论黑奴之悲惨境遇》（A Caution to Great Britain and Her Colonies, Concerning the Calamitous State of the Enslaved Negroes）之大作：我们这群卑微、受压迫、匮乏且饱受摧折屈辱之黑人子民，谨以此感恩呈文晋谒诸位尊前，致以我们肺腑深处之挚爱与最崇高之感激；由衷铭感贵会为砸碎奴役枷锁所彰显之仁善厚德、不懈操劳与慈悲斡旋，并为成千上万受苦受难、身负过重枷锁之黑人兄弟同胞带来些许抚慰与释怀。
> 诸位仁人君子，若承蒙上天眷顾，贵会之坚韧不拔终能借由上帝洪恩在任何微小程度上减轻受难者之沉重枷锁，这毫无疑问必将在上帝手中成为可能感化拯救诸多压迫者灵魂之神圣契机；果真如此，我们深信那位双目时刻眷顾一切受造物、永远奖赏一切真善美德、垂顾受屈者呼求之独一真神，必赐予诸位及其子孙后裔凡人所无法言表或构想之属天福祉，这亦正是我们身为主权沦丧、饱受压迫与屈辱之子民，最为热切祈愿与代求者。

===Original===

These gentlemen received us very kindly, with a promise to exert themselves on behalf of the oppressed Africans, and we parted.
While in town I chanced once to be invited to a quaker’s wedding. The simple and yet expressive mode used at their solemnizations is worthy of note. The following is the true form of it:
After the company have met they have seasonable exhortations by several of the members; the bride and bridegroom stand up, and, taking each other by the hand in a solemn manner, the man audily declares to this purpose:
“Friends, in the fear of the Lord, and in the presence of this assembly, whom I desire to be my witnesses, I take this my friend, M. N. to be my wife; promising, through divine assistance, to be unto her a loving and faithful husband till death separate us:” and the woman makes the like declaration. Then the two first sign their names to the record, and as many more witnesses as have a mind. I had the honour to subscribe mine to a register in Gracechurch-Court, Lombard-Street.
We returned to London in August; and our ship not going immediately to sea, I shipped as a steward in an American ship called the *Harmony*, Captain John Willet, and left London in March 1786, bound to Philadelphia. Eleven days after sailing we carried our foremast away. We had a nine weeks passage, which caused our trip not to succeed well, the market for our goods proving bad; and, to make it worse, my commander began to play me the like tricks as others too often practise on free negroes in the West Indies. But I thank God I found many friends here, who in some measure prevented him. On my return to London in August I was very agreeably surprised to find that the benevolence of government had adopted the plan of some philanthropic individuals to send the Africans from hence to their native quarter; and that some vessels were then engaged to carry them to Sierra Leone; an act which redounded to the honour of all concerned in its promotion, and filled me with prayers and much rejoicing. There was then in the city a select committee of gentlemen for the black poor, to some of whom I had the honour of being known; and, as soon as they heard of my arrival they sent for me to the committee.

===Chinese===

这些绅士极尽仁厚地接纳了我们，郑重许诺将竭尽全力为受压迫之非洲人（Africans）抗争到底，随后我们依依惜别。

在城期间我偶蒙盛情受邀出席一场贵格会之婚礼。彼等在婚礼大典上所行之简朴却意涵深邃之仪轨，极其值得铭记。其真实仪轨如下：

全堂亲友齐聚后，数名会友先后作应时得体之劝勉教诲；新郎新娘庄严并立，彼此执手肃穆相向，新郎当众朗声宣誓道：

“诸位信友，在敬畏上主之中，并当着在座会众之面（我祈愿诸位作我见证），我迎娶这位挚友 M. N. 为我的妻子；我誓愿借着神的护佑恩典，作她亲爱忠贞之夫君，直至死亡将我们分离：”新娘亦依此作出相同之誓词。随即新郎新娘二人率先在证书上签署大名，在座愿意作证之宾朋亦随之联署。我有幸在伦巴第街恩典教堂苑（Gracechurch-Court, Lombard-Street）之婚书登记册上，亲笔签下了自己的名字。

我们于 8 月返抵伦敦；因我船未能立刻起航出海，我受聘在一艘名为“和谐号”（Harmony）之美国海船充任管事侍从，该船由约翰·威利特船长（Captain John Willet）统领，于 1786 年 3 月起航离英开赴费城。出海 11 天前桅折断。此行航程足足耗费 9 周之久，导致此番航程大受挫折，货物市道极度萧条；尤有甚者，我的统领船长竟开始向我重施西印度群岛（West Indies）虐待自由黑人之无赖恶技。然而感谢上帝，我在当地觅得甚多仗义执言之知友，在一定程度上阻遏了彼之凶焰。8 月重返伦敦之际，令我大感惊喜的是，英国政府本其慈悲仁政，采纳了数位慈善人士之方略，决定出资将本地之非洲流亡同胞遣送返乡；并已包租数艘海船运送彼等前往塞拉利昂（Sierra Leone）；此等善举令所有赞襄促成者名垂青史，亦令我心充满欢喜称颂与祈祷。当时伦敦城内设有一由绅士名流组成之救济贫困黑人特别委员会，我有幸与其中数位绅士相识；彼等一闻我归国之讯，立遣人召我前往委员会共商大计。

===Original===

When I came there they informed me of the intention of government; and as they seemed to think me qualified to superintend part of the undertaking, they asked me to go with the black poor to Africa. I pointed out to them many objections to my going; and particularly I expressed some difficulties on the account of the slave dealers, as I would certainly oppose their traffic in the human species by every means in my power. However these objections were overruled by the gentlemen of the committee, who prevailed on me to go, and recommended me to the honourable Commissioners of his Majesty’s Navy as a proper person to act as commissary for government in the intended expedition; and they accordingly appointed me in November 1786 to that office, and gave me sufficient power to act for the government in the capacity of commissary, having received my warrant and the following order.

> By the principal Officers and Commissioners of his Majesty’s Navy.
> Whereas you were directed, by our warrant of the 4th of last month, to receive into your charge from Mr. Irving the surplus provisions remaining of what was provided for the voyage, as well as the provisions for the support of the black poor, after the landing at Sierra Leone, with the clothing, tools, and all other articles provided at government’s expense; and as the provisions were laid in at the rate of two months for the voyage, and for four months after the landing, but the number embarked being so much less than was expected, whereby there may be a considerable surplus of provisions, clothing, etc. These are, in addition to former orders, to direct and require you to appropriate or dispose of such surplus to the best advantage you can for the benefit of government, keeping and rendering to us a faithful account of what you do herein. And for your guidance in preventing any white persons going, who are not intended to have the indulgences of being carried thither, we send you herewith a list of those recommended by the Committee for the black poor as proper persons to be permitted to embark, and acquaint you that you are not to suffer any others to go who do not produce a certificate from the committee for the black poor, of their having their permission for it. For which this shall be your warrant. Dated at the Navy Office, January 16, 1787.
> **J. Hinslow**,

**Geo. Marsh**,

===Chinese===

待我抵埠，彼等向我通报了政府之意旨；因彼等深信我有才干督率该项大业之一隅，遂诚邀我随同贫困黑人重返非洲。我向彼等坦陈了自身诸多顾虑与阻碍；尤为严正指出奴隶贩子必成祸患，因我誓必倾尽所能全力抵制这等贩卖人口之罪恶勾当。然而委员会诸位绅士排除了所有异议，苦劝我受命随行，并将我举荐予英皇陛下尊贵之皇家海军事务专员公署（Commissioners of his Majesty’s Navy），举荐我为在拟议远征探险中代表政府执掌粮饷军需之适宜人选；公署遂于 1786 年 11 月正式委任我履任该职，并授予我充分权限以军需粮饷官（commissary）之身份代行政府职责，我领受了委任状与如下训令：

> 钦奉皇家海军主管官员暨专员公署训令。
> 鉴于公署已于上月 4 日签发委任训令，责成尔自欧文先生处接收航程备办用余之剩余物资，并接收塞拉利昂登岸后用于救济贫困黑人之粮食储备，以及由政府出资备办之衣着、工器具及一切其他杂项物资；鉴于备储粮食乃按航程 2 个月、登岸后供养 4 个月之配额采办，然实际登船人丁远少于预期，致使粮食衣物等物资将产生丰厚结余。特此颁发追加训令，严令尔务必将此等结余物资以最符合政府利益之最善方式妥善处分或调配，并就尔经手之诸般账目向公署呈报忠实详尽之报告。且为指引尔杜绝任何未经恩准之白人擅自随船前往，特随函附呈救济贫困黑人特别委员会所推荐准予登船人员之名册乙份，并郑重告诫尔，凡未能出具特别委员会签署准其登船之证明文书者，尔一概严禁放行登船。特颁此令，此令即为尔之授权凭据。钦命于海军事务公署，1787 年 1 月 16 日。
> J. 欣斯洛（J. Hinslow）
> 乔治·马什（Geo. Marsh）

===Original===

**W. Palmer**.
> To Mr. Gustavus Vassa, Commissary

of Provisions and Stores for the

Black Poor going to Sierra Leone.

===Chinese===

> W. 帕尔默（W. Palmer）
> 致：古斯塔夫斯·瓦萨（Gustavus Vassa）先生，
> 赴塞拉利昂贫困黑人粮食军需监理官（Commissary of Provisions and Stores for the Black Poor going to Sierra Leone）

===Original===

I proceeded immediately to the execution of my duty on board the vessels destined for the voyage, where I continued till the March following.
During my continuance in the employment of government, I was struck with the flagrant abuses committed by the agent, and endeavoured to remedy them, but without effect. One instance, among many which I could produce, may serve as a specimen. Government had ordered to be provided all necessaries (slops, as they are called, included) for 750 persons; however, not being able to muster more than 426, I was ordered to send the superfluous slops, etc. to the king’s stores at Portsmouth; but, when I demanded them for that purpose from the agent, it appeared they had never been bought, though paid for by government. But that was not all, government were not the only objects of peculation; these poor people suffered infinitely more; their accommodations were most wretched; many of them wanted beds, and many more clothing and other necessaries. For the truth of this, and much more, I do not seek credit from my own assertion. I appeal to the testimony of Capt. Thompson, of the *Nautilus*, who convoyed us, to whom I applied in February 1787 for a remedy, when I had remonstrated to the agent in vain, and even brought him to be a witness of the injustice and oppression I complained of. I appeal also to a letter written by these wretched people, so early as the beginning of the preceding January, and published in the *Morning Herald* of the 4th of that month, signed by twenty of their chiefs.
I could not silently suffer government to be thus cheated, and my countrymen plundered and oppressed, and even left destitute of the necessaries for almost their existence. I therefore informed the Commissioners of the Navy of the agent’s proceeding; but my dismission was soon after procured, by means of a gentleman in the city, whom the agent, conscious of his peculation, had deceived by letter, and whom, moreover, empowered the same agent to receive on board, at the government expense, a number of persons as passengers, contrary to the orders I received. By this I suffered a considerable loss in my property: however, the commissioners were satisfied with my conduct, and wrote to Capt. Thompson, expressing their approbation of it.

===Chinese===

我当即登临指定用于此番航程之海船恪尽厥职，履任直至次年 3 月。

在为政府效力任职期间，我深为负责代办该项事务之代理官吏所犯之猖獗贪渎暴行所震撼，并曾竭力图谋矫正除弊，奈何徒劳无功。在我所能出具之诸多铁证中，特举一例足资为证。政府明令为 750 人备齐一切生活必需物资（包括俗称之成衣被服）；然而因实际召集集结之人丁仅有 426 人，我奉命将剩余结余之被服成衣运送移交至朴茨茅斯（Portsmouth）皇家仓库备查；然而当我为此向代理官员索取物资之际，真相赫然大白：此等物资虽早已全额由政府据实拨付公款，却根本从未采购！然而不仅如此，政府亦非唯一遭贪墨中饱之受害者；这群可怜无辜之同胞所受惨痛更千百倍于此；彼等之起居条件凄惨绝伦；众人皆缺乏铺板被褥，更有大批人丁衣不蔽体缺乏各色必需用度。为证此言非虚，我绝非单凭一己之辞博取世人信服。我诉诸护航舰队“鹦鹉螺号”（Nautilus）战舰汤普森舰长（Capt. Thompson）之公开证言，当我向贪渎代理官抗争无果后，曾于 1787 年 2 月求告于汤普森舰长以期匡正除弊，甚至特意引其登船亲眼目睹我所控诉之不义暴行。我亦诉诸这群罹难同胞早在当年 1 月初亲笔撰写并刊载于 1 月 4 日《晨报》（Morning Herald）之公开控诉信，信末赫然联署有彼等 20 位酋长之亲笔签名。

我绝无法坐视政府遭此欺诈中饱，坐视同胞惨遭掳掠压迫，甚至连维系性命之基本口粮被服皆被盘剥殆尽。我遂向皇家海军事务专员公署如实揭发代理官员之贪腐罪行；孰料我之撤职解聘令未几便被暗箱操作迅速下达，此乃伦敦城内某位绅士从中作梗所致，该代理官深知贪渎败露，以书信巧言蒙蔽该绅士，该绅士甚至反常赋权同一代理官，违背我所领受之严令，由政府出资大肆安排闲杂人等登船为客。借此阴谋我在个人资财上蒙受了惨重折损：然而皇家海军事务专员皆对我的操守品格深表钦佩与满意，并特意致信汤普森舰长，对我的公忠体国之举予以高度褒扬。

===Original===

Thus provided, they proceeded on their voyage; and at last, worn out by treatment, perhaps not the most mild, and wasted by sickness, brought on by want of medicine, cloths, bedding, etc. they reached Sierra Leone just at the commencement of the rains. At that season of the year it is impossible to cultivate the lands; their provisions therefore were exhausted before they could derive any benefit from agriculture; and it is not surprising that many, especially the lascars, whose constitutions are very tender, and who had been cooped up in ships from October to June, and accommodated in the manner I have mentioned, should be so wasted by their confinement as not long to survive it.
Thus ended my part of the long-talked-of expedition to Sierra Leone; an expedition which, however unfortunate in the event, was humane and politic in its design, nor was its failure owing to government: everything was done on their part; but there was evidently sufficient mismanagement attending the conduct and execution of it to defeat its success.
I should not have been so ample in my account of this transaction, had not the share I bore in it been made the subject of partial animadversion, and even my dismission from my employment thought worthy of being made by some a matter of public triumph.24 The motives which might influence any person to descend to a petty contest with an obscure African, and to seek gratification by his depression, perhaps it is not proper here to inquire into or relate, even if its detection were necessary to my vindication; but I thank Heaven it is not. I wish to stand by my own integrity, and not to shelter myself under the impropriety of another; and I trust the behaviour of the Commissioners of the Navy to me entitle me to make this assertion; for after I had been dismissed, March 24, I drew up a memorial thus:

===Chinese===

在这般克扣匮乏之供应下，海船开赴大洋；历经并非最为仁慈之苛刻虐待与折磨，在缺医少药、无衣无被之凄凉境况下饱受疫病摧残，彼等安抵塞拉利昂之际，恰值暴雨倾盆之雨季启幕。在一年中的该季节根本无法开垦荒地；因之彼等之微薄口粮在尚未自农耕中获得分毫收益之前便已告罄绝粮；因之许多人——尤其是那些体质羸弱不堪、自 10 月直至翌年 6 月整整大半年被禁锢围困在船舱中、饱受上述惨境折磨之拉斯卡尔（lascars，东印度水手）——在长时监禁摧折下未能熬过雨季暴病身亡，实不足为怪。

至此，我在这桩久经热议之塞拉利昂远征大业中所分担之使命终告落幕；这场远征尽管结局不幸，其最初之宏图初衷却尽显仁道与远见卓识，其溃败亦绝非归咎于英国政府：政府方面已仁至义尽穷尽其责；然在具体执行与统筹指挥中，显然充斥着足以彻底摧毁整个大业之骇人渎职与腐败混乱。

若非我在此项使命中所分担之职责往昔曾遭怀有偏见之小人恶意攻讦，甚至我的解职卸任竟被某些宵小视为值得公开夸耀庆祝之胜利（见附注 24），我断不致在这桩变故上泼墨如此详尽。至于某些人出于何种险恶动机，竟不惜屈尊降贵与一个籍籍无名之非洲黑人展开卑鄙纠缠，并借踩踏迫害我以博取病态之快感，在此或许无须细究深挖，纵然揭露此等阴谋对我之自我辩白大有裨益；然我感谢苍天，我之清白无须仰赖揭露小人而立。我愿端立于自身之操守清廉之中，而不屑于借他人之不义恶行以自蔽；我深信皇家海军事务专员公署待我之公道礼遇，足以赋予我作出此番宣告之底气；因在我于 3 月 24 日接获解职文书后，我特意起草呈递了如下呈请申冤公文：

===Original===

> Humbly showeth,
> That your Lordships’ memorialist was, by the Honourable the Commissioners of his Majesty’s Navy, on the 4th of December last, appointed to the above employment by warrant from that board;
> That he accordingly proceeded to the execution of his duty on board of the *Vernon*, being one of the ships appointed to proceed to Africa with the above poor;
> That your memorialist, to his great grief and astonishment, received a letter of dismission from the Honourable Commissioners of the Navy, by your Lordships’ orders;
> That, conscious of having acted with the most perfect fidelity and the greatest assiduity in discharging the trust reposed in him, he is altogether at a loss to conceive the reasons of your Lordships’ having altered the favourable opinion you were pleased to conceive of him, sensible that your Lordships would not proceed to so severe a measure without some apparent good cause; he therefore has every reason to believe that his conduct has been grossly misrepresented to your Lordships; and he is the more confirmed in his opinion, because, by opposing measures of others concerned in the same expedition, which tended to defeat your Lordships’ humane intentions, and to put the government to a very considerable additional expense, he created a number of enemies, whose misrepresentations, he has too much reason to believe, laid the foundation of his dismission. Unsupported by friends, and unaided by the advantages of a liberal education, he can only hope for redress from the justice of his cause, in addition to the mortification of having been removed from his employment, and the advantage which he reasonably might have expected to have derived therefrom. He has had the misfortune to have sunk a considerable part of his little property in fitting himself out, and in other expenses arising out of his situation, an account of which he here annexes. Your memorialist will not trouble your Lordships with a vindication of any part of his conduct, because he knows not of what crimes he is accused; he, however, earnestly entreats that you will be pleased to direct an inquiry into his behaviour during the time he acted in the public service; and, if it be found that his dismission arose from false representations, he is confident that in your Lordships’ justice he shall find redress.

===Chinese===

> 谨呈文卑微申明：
> 阁下呈文人蒙皇家海军事务专员公署于去年 12 月 4 日签发委任训令，荣任上述公职差役；
> 呈文人遂恪尽职守登临“弗农号”（Vernon）海船执行公务，该船系奉派运送上述贫困黑人开赴非洲之海船之一；
> 呈文人惊悉万分悲痛，顷接遵奉阁下台令由海军事务专员公署签发之解职文书；
> 呈文人深知自身在履行托付之公职中，始终秉持最完美之忠诚与最勤勉之操劳，实莫测阁下何以猝然收回往昔所赐之青睐与厚誉，深知阁下若非事出有因绝不致断然采取此等严厉惩戒之举；呈文人因此有充分理由深信，自身之行止遭到了小人极度歪曲之恶意构陷；且呈文人之所以对此确信无疑，乃因呈文人在远征过程中，坚决抵制参与同航之他人妄图破坏阁下仁慈宏愿、并陷政府于巨额额外支出之贪渎恶行，因之树敌甚多，呈文人有充分证据确信，正是此辈宵小之恶意诬告中伤，构成了撤销呈文人公职之根由。呈文人无权贵奥援，亦缺乏博雅博学教育之优势，在承受无端罢黜之耻辱、以及合理期许从中获益之幻灭之余，唯有诉诸一己诉求之天理正义。呈文人更不幸在整备远征行装及因履行公职所招致之各项开支中，垫付折损了微薄积蓄之大部，附呈开销明细账目乙份。呈文人不敢以冗词辩白烦扰阁下清听，因呈文人至今不知身犯何罪；然而，呈文人热切恳请阁下恩准立案彻查呈文人在供职公门期间之一切行止；若经查实呈文人之罢黜纯属源自诬告诬陷，呈文人深信在阁下之至高公义之中，必能昭雪平反伸张正义。

===Original===

> Your petitioner therefore humbly prays that your Lordships will take his case into consideration, and that you will be pleased to order payment of the above referred-to account, amounting to £32 4s. and also the wages intended, which is most humbly submitted.
> London, May 12, 1787.

The above petition was delivered into the hands of their Lordships, who were kind enough, in the space of some few months afterwards, without hearing, to order me 50£ sterling﻿—that is, 18£ wages for the time (upwards of four months) I acted a faithful part in their service. Certainly the sum is more than a free negro would have had in the western colonies!!!
March the 21st, 1788, I had the honour of presenting the Queen with a petition on behalf of my African brethren, which was received most graciously by her Majesty:25

===Chinese===

> 因之呈请人卑微叩请阁下体察实情，俯准核销支付上述附呈之账目欠款，计 32 镑 4 先令（£32 4s.），并补发应得薪饷，以上谨呈，伏乞裁夺。
> 伦敦，1787 年 5 月 12 日。

上述请愿书递交至诸位专员大臣手中，彼等极尽仁厚，在数月之后未加传讯听证，便特意批示赔偿我 50 英镑现银（50£）——亦即包含补发我在其麾下竭诚供职期间（4 个多月）应得之 18 英镑薪饷（18£）。这笔款项确乎远超一个自由黑人在西部殖民地所能期许者！！！

1788 年 3 月 21 日，我有幸代表非洲弟兄向王后陛下当面呈递了一份请愿书，蒙王后陛下极其慈悲温厚接纳（见附注 25）：

===Original===

> To the Queen’s most Excellent Majesty:
> **Madam**﻿—Your Majesty’s well known benevolence and humanity emboldens me to approach your royal presence, trusting that the obscurity of my situation will not prevent your Majesty from attending to the sufferings for which I plead.
> Yet I do not solicit your royal pity for my own distress; my sufferings, although numerous, are in a measure forgotten. I supplicate your Majesty’s compassion for millions of my African countrymen, who groan under the lash of tyranny in the West Indies.
> The oppression and cruelty exercised to the unhappy negroes there, have at length reached the British legislature, and they are now deliberating on its redress; even several persons of property in slaves in the West Indies, have petitioned parliament against its continuance, sensible that it is as impolitic as it is unjust﻿—and what is inhuman must ever be unwise.
> Your Majesty’s reign has been hitherto distinguished by private acts of benevolence and bounty; surely the more extended the misery is, the greater claim it has to your Majesty’s compassion, and the greater must be your Majesty’s pleasure in administering to its relief.
> I presume, therefore, gracious Queen, to implore your interposition with your royal consort, in favour of the wretched Africans; that, by your Majesty’s benevolent influence, a period may now be put to their misery; and that they may be raised from the condition of brutes, to which they are at present degraded, to the rights and situation of freemen, and admitted to partake of the blessings of your Majesty’s happy government; so shall your Majesty enjoy the heartfelt pleasure of procuring happiness to millions, and be rewarded in the grateful prayers of themselves, and of their posterity.
> And may the all-bountiful Creator shower on your Majesty, and the Royal Family, every blessing that this world can afford, and every fullness of joy which divine revelation has promised us in the next.
> I am your Majesty’s most dutiful and devoted servant to command,

**Gustavus Vassa**,

The Oppressed Ethiopean.
> No. 53, Baldwin’s Gardens.

===Chinese===

> 呈王后至尊陛下御览：
> 启禀王后陛下：陛下素享盛誉之仁慈博爱与崇高人道，鼓舞呈文人冒昧晋谒至尊御前，深信呈文人出身之微贱不致阻碍陛下垂听这番由衷代求之血泪呼声。
> 呈文人非为自身之苦难叩求陛下怜恤；呈文人平生所历苦难虽重，如今大多已渐归淡忘。呈文人乃是为数百万在西印度群岛（West Indies）暴政皮鞭下呻吟泣血之非洲同胞叩求陛下之皇恩浩荡。
> 彼处不幸黑奴所受之惨无人道与残酷摧残，终已传入英国立法机关圣听，议会如今正在研商匡正除弊之策；甚至西印度群岛多位拥有黑奴私产之庄园主，亦深明此等暴政既不义亦非良策（凡灭绝人性者断非明智之举），联名向议会请愿废止此制。
> 陛下之治世至今素以私德彰显之仁善厚施名垂青史；苦难愈是深重辽阔，愈当祈求陛下之浩荡慈悲，陛下在抚平苦难之中所获之圣悦亦必愈发高远。
> 承蒙皇恩，至尊王后，呈文人冒昧叩请陛下在君王身侧慈悲斡旋，庇护这群悲惨可怜之非洲子民；借由陛下仁爱之懿德德音，使彼等之苦难终告终结；使彼等脱离如今沉沦之畜类惨境，晋升享有自由民之同等权利与尊严，并得蒙庇佑同享陛下仁政之福祉；如此陛下必享泽被数百万生灵之无上至福，并千秋万代永享彼等及其子孙后代由衷感恩之祈福。
> 愿至丰至盛之造物主，将今世所能赐予之一切福祉，以及圣言启示在来世所许诺之丰盛永恒狂喜，沛然倾注于陛下及皇家皇室尊身。
> 呈文人谨恪奉御旨，作陛下至诚顺服之忠仆，
> 古斯塔夫斯·瓦萨（Gustavus Vassa）
> 受压迫的埃塞俄比亚人（The Oppressed Ethiopean）
> 鲍德温花园 53 号（No. 53, Baldwin’s Gardens）

===Original===

The negro consolidated act, made by the assembly of Jamaica last year, and the new act of amendment now in agitation there, contain a proof of the existence of those charges that have been made against the planters relative to the treatment of their slaves.
I hope to have the satisfaction of seeing the renovation of liberty and justice resting on the British government, to vindicate the honour of our common nature. These are concerns which do not perhaps belong to any particular office: but, to speak more seriously to every man of sentiment, actions like these are the just and sure foundation of future fame; a reversion, though remote, is coveted by some noble minds as a substantial good. It is upon these grounds that I hope and expect the attention of gentlemen in power. These are designs consonant to the elevation of their rank, and the dignity of their stations: they are ends suitable to the nature of a free and generous government; and, connected with views of empire and dominion, suited to the benevolence and solid merit of the legislature. It is a pursuit of substantial greatness.﻿—May the time come﻿—at least the speculation to me is pleasing﻿—when the sable people shall gratefully commemorate the auspicious era of extensive freedom. Then shall those persons26 particularly be named with praise and honour, who generously proposed and stood forth in the cause of humanity, liberty, and good policy; and brought to the ear of the legislature designs worthy of royal patronage and adoption. May Heaven make the British senators the dispersers of light, liberty, and science, to the uttermost parts of the earth: then will be glory to God on the highest, on earth peace, and goodwill to men:﻿—Glory, honour, peace, etc. to every soul of man that worketh good, to the Britons first, (because to them the Gospel is preached) and also to the nations. “Those that honour their Maker have mercy on the poor.” “It is righteousness exalteth a nation; but sin is a reproach to any people; destruction shall be to the workers of iniquity, and the wicked shall fall by their own wickedness.” May the blessings of the Lord be upon the heads of all those who commiserated the cases of the oppressed negroes, and the fear of God prolong their days; and may their expectations be filled with gladness! “The liberal devise liberal things, and by liberal things shall stand,” Isaiah 32:8.

===Chinese===

牙买加议会去年所颁布之《黑人统合法案》（negro consolidated act），以及目前在彼处酝酿商讨之新修正案，皆成了指控种植园主虐待奴隶罪责确凿无疑之铁证。

我殷切期盼能有幸目睹自由与正义在英国政府之庇护下重获新生，以洗雪我们人类共同天性所蒙受之耻辱。此等伟业或许并非专属某一特定衙署之职权：然肃穆而言，对于天下每一位深明大义之良知君子，此等善行乃是奠定万古流芳令名之坚实基石；一笔丰厚遗产（哪怕遥不可及）亦被崇高心灵视为不可或缺之真切福祉。正因基于此等信念，我热切期盼执掌权柄之衮衮诸公予以垂注。此等宏图伟略契合彼等之显赫门第，相称于彼等之尊崇官位：契合自由宽厚政府之本色；且若与帝国与统治之远大抱负相融，尤能彰显立法者之深仁厚德与坚实勋业。这乃是对真正伟大境界之崇高追求。愿那一天早日降临——至少于我而言这番憧憬令人陶醉——届时黑肤族民（sable people）必将满怀谢忱，永远铭记自由浩荡铺展之吉祥纪元。届时，那些慷慨解囊、挺身而出捍卫人性、自由与良政大业之人（见附注 26），那些将相称于皇家庇护采纳之宏图盛策送达立法机关圣听之人，必将受到万民之特别颂扬与敬仰。愿上天恩赐英国议员，使其化为向地极万方播撒光明、自由与科学之使者：届时在至高之处荣耀归与神，在地上平安归与人！——荣耀、尊贵、平安归于一切行善之人，先是赐予不列颠人（因福音首先向彼等宣讲），亦赐予天下万国万民。“敬重造物主的，必怜悯贫穷人。”“公义使邦国高举；罪恶是人民的羞辱；作恶的必遭倾覆，恶人必因自己的恶行跌倒。”愿主的浩荡祝福常在一切怜悯受压迫黑人之人的头上，敬畏神的心必延年益寿；愿彼等之期许充盈属天喜乐！“高明人却谋高明事，在高明事上也必常立”，见《以赛亚书》32:8（Isaiah 32:8）。

===Original===

They can say with pious Job, “Did not I weep for him that was in trouble? was not my soul grieved for the poor?” Job 30:25.
As the inhuman traffic of slavery is to be taken into the consideration of the British legislature, I doubt not, if a system of commerce was established in Africa, the demand for manufactures would most rapidly augment, as the native inhabitants will insensibly adopt the British fashions, manners, customs, etc. In proportion to the civilization, so will be the consumption of British manufactures.
The wear and tear of a continent, nearly twice as large as Europe, and rich in vegetable and mineral productions, is much easier conceived than calculated.
A case in point.﻿—It cost the Aborigines of Britain little or nothing in clothing, etc. The difference between their forefathers and the present generation, in point of consumption, is literally infinite. The supposition is most obvious. It will be equally immense in Africa﻿—The same cause, viz. civilization, will ever have the same effect.
It is trading upon safe grounds. A commercial intercourse with Africa opens an inexhaustible source of wealth to the manufacturing interests of Great Britain, and to all which the slave trade is an objection.
If I am not misinformed, the manufacturing interest is equal, if not superior, to the landed interest, as to the value, for reasons which will soon appear. The abolition of slavery, so diabolical, will give a most rapid extension of manufactures, which is totally and diametrically opposite to what some interested people assert.
The manufacturers of this country must and will, in the nature and reason of things, have a full and constant employ by supplying the African markets.
Population, the bowels and surface of Africa, abound in valuable and useful returns; the hidden treasures of centuries will be brought to light and into circulation. Industry, enterprise, and mining, will have their full scope, proportionably as they civilize. In a word, it lays open an endless field of commerce to the British manufactures and merchant adventurer. The manufacturing interest and the general interests are synonymous. The abolition of slavery would be in reality an universal good.
Tortures, murder, and every other imaginable barbarity and iniquity, are practised upon the poor slaves with impunity. I hope the slave trade will be abolished.

===Chinese===

彼等能随圣徒约伯同声宣告：“人遭难，我岂不为他哭泣呢？人穷乏，我岂不为他忧愁呢？”见《约伯记》30:25（Job 30:25）。

鉴于灭绝人性之奴隶贸易即将提请英国议会审议，我坚信，一旦在非洲建立起正规完备之通商体制，对工业制成品之需求必将迅猛激增，因当地土著将在不知不觉中逐步采纳英国之风尚、礼仪、习俗与规制。文明化推进至何等程度，对英国工业制造品之消费便将水涨船高拓展至何等规模。

试想一片几乎两倍于欧洲之辽阔大陆，蕴藏着无穷无尽之植物与矿产宝藏，其所能承载消耗之物资，构想远比计算更为轻易。

眼前便有一桩明证。——昔日不列颠之原住民（Aborigines of Britain）在衣着服饰等项上几乎分文不耗。彼等先祖与当今一代在物资消费之对比上，简直有天壤之别。此等推论显而易见。非洲大陆未来之需求亦将同样浩瀚无垠——相同之动因（即文明教化），必将产生相同之奇效。

此乃立足于稳健基石之上之正当商贸。与非洲开展商业互市，为大不列颠之制造业利益开启了一座取之不尽用之不竭之财富源泉，而罪恶之奴隶贸易恰恰是阻碍这一切之最大绊脚石。

若我所闻非虚，从价值维度衡量，制造业之利益已然与土地利益并驾齐驱，甚至犹有过之，其原委未几便将大白于天下。废除灭绝人性之恶魔奴隶制，必将促成工业制造业最迅猛之飞跃拓展，此结论与某些利益攸关之宵小所妄言者完全南辕北辙相反。

依事物之本性与公理，本国之工业制造厂商完全能够且必将借由供应非洲广袤市场，获得长久充盈之开工产能与无限商机。

人口繁衍、非洲地底与地表所蕴藏之宝藏，蕴藏着无价而丰厚之回报；千百年沉睡之宝藏必将重见天日投入流通。工业、拓荒与采矿必将随着文明教化之普及，获得纵情驰骋之广阔舞台。简而言之，它为英国制造工业与开拓进取之商贾探险家，开辟了一片无边无垠之商贸蓝海。制造业之利益与大不列颠之总体国家利益实乃休戚与共。废除奴隶制度在实质上乃是一项造福普世之莫大福祉。

对可怜无辜之奴隶施以严刑拷打、屠戮谋杀以及凡人所能想象之一切野蛮暴行与罪孽，至今竟不受任何国法惩治。我祈愿奴隶贸易彻底化为历史陈迹。

===Original===

I pray it may be an event at hand. The great body of manufacturers, uniting in the cause, will considerably facilitate and expedite it; and, as I have already stated, it is most substantially their interest and advantage, and as such the nation’s at large, (except those persons concerned in the manufacturing neck-yokes, collars, chains, handcuffs, leg-bolts, drags, thumbscrews, iron muzzles, and coffins; cats, scourges, and other instruments of torture used in the slave trade). In a short time one sentiment alone will prevail, from motives of interest as well as justice and humanity. Europe contains one hundred and twenty millions of inhabitants. Query﻿—How many millions doth Africa contain? Supposing the Africans, collectively and individually, to expend 5£ a head in raiment and furniture yearly when civilized, etc. an immensity beyond the reach of imagination!
This I conceive to be a theory founded upon facts, and therefore an infallible one. If the blacks were permitted to remain in their own country, they would double themselves every fifteen years. In proportion to such increase will be the demand for manufactures. Cotton and indigo grow spontaneously in most parts of Africa; a consideration this of no small consequence to the manufacturing towns of Great Britain. It opens a most immense, glorious, and happy prospect﻿—the clothing, etc. of a continent ten thousand miles in circumference, and immensely rich in productions of every denomination in return for manufactures.
I have only therefore to request the reader’s indulgence and conclude. I am far from the vanity of thinking there is any merit in this narrative: I hope censure will be suspended, when it is considered that it was written by one who was as unwilling as unable to adorn the plainness of truth by the colouring of imagination. My life and fortune have been extremely chequered, and my adventures various. Even those I have related are considerably abridged. If any incident in this little work should appear uninteresting and trifling to most readers, I can only say, as my excuse for mentioning it, that almost every event of my life made an impression on my mind and influenced my conduct.

===Chinese===

我祈愿这一伟绩早日成为近在咫尺之现实。全体制造工商业阶层若能同心协力投身此项正义大业，必能极大地推动并加速其进程；且诚如我前文所述，废奴在实质上乃是彼等至关重要之切身利益与优势所在，因之亦是大不列颠整个民族之福祉所在（除却那些专门制造奴隶贸易所用之颈枷、项圈、铁链、手铐、脚镣、铁钩、手铐夹、铁口罩、囚笼铁棺、九尾鞭、皮鞭以及其他施酷刑刑具之行当除外）。未几，不论出于切身利害考量，抑或出于正义与人道之呼唤，一种崇高之共识必将君临天下。欧洲拥有一亿二千万（120,000,000）居民。试问——非洲又蕴藏多少百万之生灵？假定非洲人民一旦开化归正，每人每年在服饰与器皿器具上仅消费 5 英镑（5£），其汇聚之总量便庞大至超乎凡人想象之极限！

我深信这是一套完全奠基于客观事实之上之真知灼见，因而是坚不可摧之真理。黑人若得准许安居故土，人口每 15 年便将翻上一番。随着人口繁衍，对制造品之需求亦将水涨船高。棉花与蓝靛在非洲多数疆域自然繁茂滋长；对于大不列颠各大工业制造名城而言，这乃是一笔至关重要之战略资源。它展现了一幅恢宏、荣美且无比幸福之广阔前景——为一片周长达一万英里（10,000 英里）之浩瀚大陆披上衣裳，该大陆出产之各色丰饶物产更将作为交换回报，源源不断输往本国换取工业制成品。

因之我唯有乞求读者诸君之包容宽恕，以此收束全篇。我绝无虚荣自负之妄念以为本部自传有何非凡功绩：我唯期盼世人能免除苛责，念及此书出自一个既不愿亦无能借虚妄想象之笔墨粉饰朴素真理之作者手笔。我的生平与际遇极其跌宕起伏，所历险阻千奇百怪。即便是文中已然叙及者，亦经过了大幅删繁就简。这部拙作之中若有任何轶事在多数读者眼中显得平淡无奇琐碎微末，我唯能以此为辩白由衷申明：我生平之几乎每一次遭遇，皆在我的心灵深处留下了深刻烙印，并塑造了我一生的操守作为。

===Original===

I early accustomed myself to look for the hand of God in the minutest occurrence, and to learn from it a lesson of morality and religion; and in this light every circumstance I have related was to me of importance. After all, what makes any event important, unless by its observation we become better and wiser, and learn “to do justly, to love mercy, and to walk humbly before God?” To those who are possessed of this spirit, there is scarcely any book or incident so trifling that does not afford some profit, while to others the experience of ages seems of no use; and even to pour out to them the treasures of wisdom is throwing the jewels of instruction away.

===Chinese===

我早年便已养成一种习惯，在哪怕最微末之琐事之中寻觅上帝冥冥之巨手，并从中汲取道德伦理与宗教信仰之训诲；从这重维度考量，我所记述之每一桩际遇于我而言皆重逾千钧。归根结底，尘世间之任何事变何以称得上至关重要，除非借由对其省察，我们能变得更为良善睿智，并懂得“行公义，好怜悯，存谦卑的心与神同行”？对于身怀此等崇高品格之人而言，世间几乎寻不出任何一本书籍或细微琐事不能带来些许造就与益处；然而在他人眼中，历代之宏富经验亦浑如无用废纸；向此辈凡夫俗子倾倒智慧之宝藏，无异于将训诲之真珠浪掷于泥淖之中。
